we obtain
\hat{\beta}\sim\frac{1}{T}
.

Bound of the term
⑩
.

We need to choose hyperparameters such that

\frac{2\tau\hat{\beta}^{3}c\delta_{\rm byz}\hat{a}^{2}K}{81L\sqrt{L\Delta}}+\frac{2\tau^{3}\hat{\beta}^{3}c\delta_{\rm byz}T^{2}K}{81L\sqrt{L\Delta}}\leq\frac{\Delta}{24}.

By choosing
\hat{\beta}
such that

\displaystyle\hat{\beta}\leq\min\left\{\left(\frac{81(L\Delta)^{3/2}}{96c\delta_{\rm byz}\hat{a}^{2}\tau T}\right)^{1/3},\left(\frac{81(L\Delta)^{3/2}}{96c\delta_{\rm byz}\tau^{3}T^{3}}\right)^{1/3}\right\},\quad\text{ and }\quad K\leq T+1

(80)

we have

\frac{2\tau\hat{\beta}^{3}c\delta_{\rm byz}\hat{a}^{2}K}{81L\sqrt{L\Delta}}+\frac{2\tau^{3}\hat{\beta}^{3}c\delta_{\rm byz}T^{2}K}{81L\sqrt{L\Delta}}\leq\frac{\Delta}{48}+\frac{\Delta}{48}=\frac{\Delta}{24}.

Final probability.

Therefore, the probability event

\Omega\coloneqq E^{K}\cap\overline{\Theta}^{K+1}\cap\left(\cap_{i\in\mathcal{G}}\overline{\Theta}^{K+1}_{i}\right)\cap\overline{N}^{K+1}\cap\left(\cap_{i\in\mathcal{G}}\overline{N}^{K+1}_{i}\right)\cap E_{\text{①}}\cap E_{\text{②}}\cap E_{\text{③}}\cap E_{\text{④}}\cap E_{\text{⑤}}\cap E_{\text{⑥}}\cap E_{\text{⑦}}\cap E_{\text{⑧}}\cap E_{\text{⑨}}\cap E_{\text{⑩}},

where each
E_{\text{①}}
-
E_{\text{⑩}}
denotes that each of
2
-
8
-th terms is smaller than
\frac{\Delta}{8}
and each of the terms
1,9,10
is smaller than
\frac{\Delta}{24}
. This implies that

\text{①}+\text{②}+\text{③}+\text{④}+\text{⑤}+\text{⑥}+\text{⑦}+\text{⑧}+\text{⑨}+\text{⑩}\leq 7\cdot\frac{\Delta}{8}+3\cdot\frac{\Delta}{24}=\Delta,

i.e., condition
7
in the induction assumption holds. Moreover, this also implies that

\Phi^{K+1}\leq\Phi^{0}+\Delta\leq\Delta+\Delta=2\Delta,

i.e., condition
6
in the induction assumption holds. The probability
\mathbb{P}(E_{K+1})
can be lower bounded as follows

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(E_{K+1})

\displaystyle\geq\mathbb{P}(\Omega)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\bigg(E_{K}\cap\overline{\Theta}^{K+1}\cap\left(\cap_{i\in\mathcal{G}}\overline{\Theta}^{K+1}_{i}\right)\cap\overline{N}^{K+1}\cap\left(\cap_{i\in\mathcal{G}}\overline{N}^{K+1}_{i}\right)\cap E_{\text{①}}\cap E_{\text{②}}\cap E_{\text{③}}\cap E_{\text{④}}\cap E_{\text{⑤}}\cap E_{\text{⑥}}

\displaystyle\qquad\cap E_{\text{⑦}}\cap E_{\text{⑧}}\cap E_{\text{⑨}}\cap E_{\text{⑩}}\bigg)

\displaystyle=1-\mathbb{P}\bigg(\overline{E}_{K}\cup\Theta^{K+1}\cup\left(\cup_{i\in\mathcal{G}}\Theta^{K+1}_{i}\right)\cup N^{K+1}\left(\cup_{i\in\mathcal{G}}N^{K+1}_{i}\right)\cup\overline{E}_{\text{①}}\cup\overline{E}_{\text{②}}\cup\overline{E}_{\text{③}}\cup\overline{E}_{\text{④}}\cup\overline{E}_{\text{⑤}}\cup\overline{E}_{\text{⑥}}

\displaystyle\qquad\cup\overline{E}_{\text{⑦}}\cup\overline{E}_{\text{⑧}}\cup\overline{E}_{\text{⑨}}\cup\overline{E}_{\text{⑩}}\bigg)

\displaystyle\geq 1-\mathbb{P}(\overline{E}_{K})-\mathbb{P}(\Theta^{K+1})-\sum_{i\in\mathcal{G}}\mathbb{P}(\Theta^{K+1}_{i})-\mathbb{P}(N^{K+1})-\sum_{i\in\mathcal{G}}\mathbb{P}(N^{K+1}_{i})-\mathbb{P}(\overline{E}_{\text{①}})-\mathbb{P}(\overline{E}_{\text{②}})

\displaystyle\qquad-\mathbb{P}(\overline{E}_{\text{③}})-\mathbb{P}(\overline{E}_{\text{④}})-\mathbb{P}(\overline{E}_{\text{⑤}})-\mathbb{P}(\overline{E}_{\text{⑥}})-\mathbb{P}(\overline{E}_{\text{⑦}})-\mathbb{P}(\overline{E}_{\text{⑧}})-\mathbb{P}(\overline{E}_{\text{⑨}})-\mathbb{P}(\overline{E}_{\text{⑩}})

\displaystyle\geq 1-\frac{\alpha(K+1)}{T+1}-\frac{\alpha}{8(T+1)}-\sum_{i\in\mathcal{G}}\frac{\alpha}{8G(T+1)}-\frac{\alpha}{8(T+1)}-0-10\cdot\frac{\alpha}{20(T+1)}

\displaystyle=1-\frac{\alpha(K+2)}{T+1}.

This finalizes the transition step of induction. The result of the theorem follows by setting
K=T-1
. Indeed, from (
70
) we obtain

\displaystyle\frac{\gamma}{2}\sum_{t=0}^{K}\|\nabla f(x^{t})\|^{2}\leq\Phi^{0}-\Phi^{K+1}+\Delta\leq 2\Delta\Rightarrow\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=0}^{T-1}\|\nabla f(x^{t})\|^{2}\leq\frac{4\Delta}{\gamma T}.

(81)

Final rate.

We highlight that we are interested in the functional dependency of the rate on the problem constants. Therefore, in the rest of the proof we omit using numerical constants. Translating momentum restrictions (
71
), (
72
), (
D
), (
D
), (
75
), (
77
), (
76
), and (
78
) to the step-size restriction using
12L\gamma=\beta
equality we get that the step-size should satisfy

\displaystyle\gamma=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{L}\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(\min\left\{\underbrace{\left(\frac{L\Delta G}{T\sigma^{2}}\right)^{1/2},\left(\frac{L\Delta\hat{\beta}^{2}\eta^{2}}{T\sigma^{2}}\right)^{1/4},\left(\frac{L\Delta\hat{\beta}\eta}{T\sigma^{2}}\right)^{1/3}}_{\text{from term }1\penalty\ \eqref{eq:step-size_bound_1}},\underbrace{\left(\frac{L\Delta\sqrt{G}\hat{\beta}\eta}{B_{\rm init}\sigma\sqrt{T}}\right)^{1/2}}_{\text{from term }2\penalty\ \eqref{eq:step-size_bound_2}},\right.\right.

\displaystyle\left.\left.\underbrace{\left(\frac{L\Delta\sqrt{G}\hat{\beta}\eta}{\sigma(\sqrt{L\Delta}+B_{\rm init}+\sigma)\sqrt{T}}\right)^{\frac{1}{3}},\left(\frac{L\Delta\sqrt{G}\eta}{\sigma\sqrt{c\delta_{\rm byz}}\tau T^{3/2}}\right)^{\frac{1}{3}}}_{\text{from term }3\penalty\ \eqref{eq:step-size_bound_3}},\underbrace{\left(\frac{L\Delta\hat{\beta}\eta\sqrt{G}}{\sigma(\sqrt{L\Delta}+B_{\rm init}+\sigma)\sqrt{T}}\right)^{1/4},\left(\frac{L\Delta\eta\sqrt{G}}{\sigma\sqrt{c\delta_{\rm byz}}T^{3/2}\tau}\right)^{1/4}}_{\text{from term }4\penalty\ \eqref{eq:step-size_bound_4}},\right.\right.

\displaystyle\left.\left.\underbrace{\left(\frac{L\Delta\hat{\beta}^{2}\eta^{2}\sqrt{G}}{\sigma(\sqrt{L\Delta}+B_{\rm init}+\sigma)\sqrt{T}}\right)^{1/3},\left(\frac{L\Delta\hat{\beta}\eta^{2}\sqrt{G}}{\sigma T^{3/2}\sqrt{c\delta_{\rm byz}}\tau}\right)^{1/3}}_{\text{from term }5\penalty\ \eqref{eq:step-size_bound_5}},\right.\right.

\displaystyle\left.\left.\underbrace{\left(\frac{L\Delta\hat{\beta}^{2}\eta^{2}\sqrt{G}}{\sigma(\sqrt{L\Delta}+B_{\rm init}+\sigma)\sqrt{T}}\right)^{1/4},\left(\frac{L\Delta\hat{\beta}\eta^{2}\sqrt{G}}{\sqrt{c\delta_{\rm byz}}\sigma\tau T^{3/2}}\right)^{1/4}}_{\text{from term }7\penalty\ \eqref{eq:step-size_bound_7}},\right.\right.

\displaystyle\left.\left.\underbrace{\left(\frac{L\Delta\sqrt{G}}{\sigma(\sqrt{L\Delta}+B_{\rm init}+\sigma)\sqrt{T}}\right)}_{\text{from term }6\penalty\ \eqref{eq:step-size_bound_6}},\underbrace{\left(\frac{L\Delta\sqrt{G}}{\sigma(\sqrt{L\Delta}+B_{\rm init}+\sigma)\sqrt{T}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}}_{\text{from term }8\penalty\ \eqref{eq:step-size_bound_8}}\right\}\right),

(82)

and

\displaystyle\hat{\beta}\leq\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(\underbrace{\left(\frac{L\Delta\sqrt{G}}{\sigma\sqrt{T}}\right)}_{\text{from term 6}\penalty\ \eqref{eq:step-size_bound_6}},\underbrace{\left(\frac{(L\Delta)^{2}\sqrt{G}}{\sigma T^{3/2}\tau^{3}}\right)^{1/2}}_{\text{from term 8}\penalty\ \eqref{eq:step-size_bound_8}},\underbrace{\frac{\sqrt{L\Delta}}{a^{2/3}T^{1/3}\tau^{1/3}}}_{\text{from term 9}\penalty\ \eqref{eq:step-size_bound_9}},\underbrace{\frac{\sqrt{L\Delta}}{(c\delta_{\rm byz})^{1/3}\hat{a}^{2/3}\tau^{1/3}T^{1/3}},\frac{\sqrt{L\Delta}}{(c\delta_{\rm byz})^{1/3}T\tau}}_{\text{from term 10}\penalty\ \eqref{eq:step-size_bound_10}}\right).

To guarantee the convergence to the neighborhood, we should choose
\hat{\beta}\sim\frac{1}{a}\sim\frac{1}{T}
. Therefore, the first two restrictions on
\hat{\beta}
will not contribute to the size of the neighborhood. Moreover, the worst power of
T
in (
D
) comes from the term
⑤
and equals
\gamma\sim\frac{1}{T^{5/6}}.
The second worst comes from terms
①
and
②
, and equals to
\gamma\sim\frac{1}{T^{3/4}}
. These terms give the rate of the form

\displaystyle\frac{L\Delta}{T}\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(\left(\frac{T\sigma^{2}}{L\Delta\hat{\beta}^{2}\eta^{2}}\right)^{1/4}+\left(\frac{B_{\rm init}\sigma\sqrt{T}}{L\Delta\sqrt{G}\hat{\beta}\eta}\right)^{1/2}+\left(\frac{\sigma(\sqrt{L\Delta}+B_{\rm init}+\sigma)\sqrt{T}}{L\Delta\sqrt{G}\hat{\beta}^{2}\eta^{2}}\right)^{1/3}+\left(\frac{\sigma\sqrt{c\delta_{\rm byz}}\tau T^{3/2}}{L\Delta\sqrt{G}\hat{\beta}\eta}\right)^{1/3}\right).

(83)

Now we need to plug in all restrictions on
\hat{\beta}
in (
83
). We remind that in total we should satisfy (up to numerical constants)

\displaystyle\hat{\beta}\leq\sqrt{L\Delta}\cdot\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(\min\left\{\frac{1}{a},\frac{1}{\sqrt{c\delta_{\rm byz}}\hat{a}},\frac{1}{a^{2/3}T^{1/3}\tau^{1/3}},\frac{1}{(c\delta_{\rm byz})^{1/3}\hat{a}^{2/3}\tau^{1/3}T^{1/3}},\frac{1}{(c\delta_{\rm byz})^{1/3}\tau T}\right\}\right).

We can simplify this constraint to

\displaystyle\hat{\beta}\leq\sqrt{L\Delta}\cdot\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(\min\left\{\frac{1}{aC},\frac{1}{a^{2/3}T^{1/3}\tau^{1/3}},\frac{1}{(c\delta_{\rm byz})^{1/3}\hat{a}^{2/3}\tau^{1/3}T^{1/3}},\frac{1}{(c\delta_{\rm byz})^{1/3}\tau T},\right\}\right),

(84)

where
C\coloneqq(1+\sqrt{G}\sqrt{c\delta_{\rm byz}})
. Let us do this one by one.

1.

\hat{\beta}\sim\frac{\sqrt{L\Delta}}{aC}
, where
a\sim\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}(\nicefrac{{\sqrt{d}\sigma_{\omega}\sqrt{T}}}{{\sqrt{G}}})
, gives the term in the rate of the form

\displaystyle\frac{L\Delta}{T}\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(\left(\frac{T\sigma^{2}}{L\Delta\hat{\beta}^{2}\eta^{2}}\right)^{1/4}+\left(\frac{B_{\rm init}\sigma\sqrt{T}}{L\Delta\sqrt{G}\hat{\beta}\eta}\right)^{1/2}+\left(\frac{\sigma(\sqrt{L\Delta}+B_{\rm init}+\sigma)\sqrt{T}}{L\Delta\sqrt{G}\hat{\beta}^{2}\eta^{2}}\right)^{1/3}+\left(\frac{\sigma\sqrt{c\delta_{\rm byz}}\tau T^{3/2}}{L\Delta\sqrt{G}\hat{\beta}\eta}\right)^{1/3}\right)

\displaystyle=\;

\displaystyle\frac{L\Delta}{T}\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(\left(\frac{B_{\rm init}^{2}C^{2}a^{2}T\sigma^{2}}{(L\Delta)^{2}\tau^{2}}\right)^{1/4}+\left(\frac{B^{2}_{\rm init}Ca\sigma T^{1/2}}{(L\Delta)^{3/2}\sqrt{G}\tau}\right)^{1/2}+\left(\frac{B_{\rm init}^{2}C^{2}a^{2}\sigma(\sqrt{L\Delta}+B_{\rm init}+\sigma)\sqrt{T}}{(L\Delta)^{2}\sqrt{G}\tau^{2}}\right)^{1/3}\right.

\displaystyle\hskip 256.0748pt\left.+\;\left(\frac{B_{\rm init}Ca\sigma\sqrt{c\delta_{\rm byz}}T^{3/2}}{(L\Delta)^{3/2}\sqrt{G}}\right)^{1/3}\right)

\displaystyle=\;

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(\left(\frac{(L\Delta)^{2}B_{\rm init}^{2}C^{2}a^{2}\sigma^{2}}{T^{3}\tau^{2}}\right)^{1/4}+\left(\frac{\sqrt{L\Delta}B^{2}_{\rm init}Ca\sigma}{T^{1/2}\sqrt{G}\tau}\right)^{1/2}+\left(\frac{L\Delta B_{\rm init}^{2}C^{2}a^{2}\sigma(\sqrt{L\Delta}+B_{\rm init}+\sigma)}{T^{5/2}\sqrt{G}\tau^{2}}\right)^{1/3}\right.

\displaystyle\hskip 256.0748pt\left.+\;\left(\frac{(L\Delta)^{3/2}B_{\rm init}Ca\sigma\sqrt{c\delta_{\rm byz}}}{\sqrt{G}T^{3/2}}\right)^{1/3}\right).

Now we use the specific form of
a\sim\nicefrac{{\sqrt{d}\sigma_{\omega}\sqrt{T}}}{{\sqrt{G}}}
to obtain the rate

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(\left(\frac{(L\Delta)^{2}B_{\rm init}^{2}C^{2}d\sigma_{\omega}^{2}\sigma^{2}}{GT^{2}\tau^{2}}\right)^{1/4}+\left(\frac{\sqrt{L\Delta}B_{\rm init}^{2}C\sqrt{d}\sigma_{\omega}\sigma}{G\tau}\right)^{1/2}\right.

\displaystyle\left.+\;\left(\frac{L\Delta B_{\rm init}^{2}C^{2}d\sigma_{\omega}^{2}\sigma(\sqrt{L\Delta}+B_{\rm init}+\sigma)}{(TG)^{3/2}\tau^{2}}\right)^{1/3}+\left(\frac{(L\Delta)^{3/2}B_{\rm init}C\sqrt{d}\sigma_{\omega}\sigma\sqrt{c\delta_{\rm byz}}\tau}{TG\tau}\right)^{1/3}\right).

(85)

Since
\sigma_{\omega}\sim\sqrt{T}
, we omit the first two terms in (
1
) as they have better dependency on
T.
The final rate in this regime is

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(\left(\frac{L\Delta B_{\rm init}^{2}C^{2}d\sigma_{\omega}^{2}\sigma(\sqrt{L\Delta}+B_{\rm init}+\sigma)}{(TG)^{3/2}\tau^{2}}\right)^{1/3}+\left(\frac{(L\Delta)^{3/2}B_{\rm init}C\sqrt{d}\sigma_{\omega}\sigma\sqrt{c\delta_{\rm byz}}\tau}{TG\tau}\right)^{1/3}\right).

(86)

2.

\hat{\beta}\sim\frac{\sqrt{L\Delta}}{C_{1}a^{2/3}T^{1/3}\tau^{1/3}}
, where
C_{1}=1+G^{1/3}(c\delta_{\rm byz})^{1/3}
(we combine cases 2 and 3 in (
84
) together to simplify calculations). We obtain

\displaystyle\frac{L\Delta}{T}\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(\left(\frac{T\sigma^{2}}{L\Delta\hat{\beta}^{2}\eta^{2}}\right)^{1/4}+\left(\frac{B_{\rm init}\sigma\sqrt{T}}{L\Delta\sqrt{G}\hat{\beta}\eta}\right)^{1/2}+\left(\frac{\sigma(\sqrt{L\Delta}+B_{\rm init}+\sigma)\sqrt{T}}{L\Delta\sqrt{G}\hat{\beta}^{2}\eta^{2}}\right)^{1/3}+\left(\frac{\sigma\sqrt{c\delta_{\rm byz}}\tau T^{3/2}}{L\Delta\sqrt{G}\hat{\beta}\eta}\right)^{1/3}\right)

\displaystyle=\;

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(\left(\frac{(L\Delta)^{2}B_{\rm init}^{2}C^{2}_{1}a^{4/3}\sigma^{2}}{T^{7/3}\tau^{4/3}}\right)^{1/4}+\left(\frac{\sqrt{L\Delta}B_{\rm init}^{2}C_{1}a^{2/3}\sigma}{T^{7/6}\sqrt{G}\tau^{2/3}}\right)^{1/2}\right.

\displaystyle\left.+\;\left(\frac{L\Delta B_{\rm init}^{2}C^{2}_{1}a^{4/3}\sigma(\sqrt{L\Delta}+B_{\rm init}+\sigma)}{T^{11/6}\sqrt{G}\tau^{4/3}}\right)^{1/3}+\left(\frac{(L\Delta)^{3/2}B_{\rm init}C_{1}a^{2/3}\sigma\sqrt{c\delta_{\rm byz}}\tau}{T^{7/6}\sqrt{G}\tau^{2/3}}\right)^{1/3}\right).

Now we use the specific form of
a\sim\nicefrac{{\sqrt{d}\sigma_{\omega}\sqrt{T}}}{{\sqrt{G}}}
to obtain the rate

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(\left(\frac{(L\Delta)^{2}B_{\rm init}^{2}C_{1}^{2}d^{2/3}\sigma_{\omega}^{4/3}\sigma^{2}}{G^{2/3}T^{5/3}\tau^{4/3}}\right)^{1/4}+\left(\frac{\sqrt{L\Delta}B_{\rm init}^{2}C_{1}d^{1/3}\sigma_{\omega}^{2/3}\sigma}{(GT)^{5/6}\tau^{2/3}}\right)^{1/2}\right.

(87)

\displaystyle+\;\left.\left(\frac{L\Delta B_{\rm init}^{2}C_{1}^{2}d^{2/3}\sigma_{\omega}^{4/3}\sigma(\sqrt{L\Delta}+B_{\rm init}+\sigma)}{(TG)^{7/6}\tau^{4/3}}\right)^{1/3}+\left(\frac{(L\Delta)^{3/2}B_{\rm init}C_{1}d^{1/3}\sigma_{\omega}^{2/3}\sigma\sqrt{c\delta_{\rm byz}}\tau}{(GT)^{5/6}\tau^{2/3}}\right)^{1/3}\right).

Since
\sigma_{\omega}\sim\sqrt{T}
, then the first term in (
87
) has better dependency on
T
than the other two, therefore we omit them. The final rate in this case is

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(\left(\frac{L\Delta B_{\rm init}^{2}C^{2}_{1}d^{2/3}\sigma_{\omega}^{4/3}\sigma(\sqrt{L\Delta}+B_{\rm init}+\sigma)}{(TG)^{7/6}\tau^{4/3}}\right)^{1/3}+\left(\frac{(L\Delta)^{3/2}B_{\rm init}Cd^{1/3}\sigma_{\omega}^{2/3}\sigma\sqrt{c\delta_{\rm byz}}\tau}{(GT)^{5/6}\tau^{2/3}}\right)^{1/3}\right).

(88)

3.

\hat{\beta}\sim\frac{\sqrt{L\Delta}}{(c\delta_{\rm byz})^{1/3}\tau T}
, gives the term in the rate of the form

\displaystyle\frac{L\Delta}{T}\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(\left(\frac{T\sigma^{2}}{L\Delta\hat{\beta}^{2}\eta^{2}}\right)^{1/4}+\left(\frac{B_{\rm init}\sigma\sqrt{T}}{L\Delta\sqrt{G}\hat{\beta}\eta}\right)^{1/2}+\left(\frac{\sigma(\sqrt{L\Delta}+B_{\rm init}+\sigma)\sqrt{T}}{L\Delta\sqrt{G}\hat{\beta}^{2}\eta^{2}}\right)^{1/3}+\left(\frac{\sigma\sqrt{c\delta_{\rm byz}}\tau T^{3/2}}{L\Delta\sqrt{G}\hat{\beta}\eta}\right)^{1/3}\right)

\displaystyle=\;

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(\left(\frac{(L\Delta)^{2}(c\delta_{\rm byz})^{2/3}B_{\rm init}^{2}\sigma^{2}}{T}\right)^{1/4}+\left(\frac{(L\Delta)^{1/2}B_{\rm init}^{2}(c\delta_{\rm byz})^{1/3}\sigma}{\sqrt{GT}}\right)^{1/2}\right.

\displaystyle\left.+\;\left(\frac{L\Delta B_{\rm init}^{2}(c\delta_{\rm byz})^{2/3}\sigma(\sqrt{L\Delta}+B_{\rm init}+\sigma)}{\sqrt{TG}}\right)^{1/3}+\left(\frac{(c\delta_{\rm byz})^{5/6}(L\Delta)^{3/2}B_{\rm init}\sigma\tau}{\sqrt{TG}}\right)^{1/3}\right).

We keep only the last two terms, since they have the worst dependence on
T
. Therefore, the final rate in this case is

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(\left(\frac{L\Delta B_{\rm init}^{2}\sigma(\sqrt{L\Delta}+B_{\rm init}+\sigma)}{\sqrt{TG}}\right)^{1/3}+\left(\frac{\sqrt{c\delta_{\rm byz}}(L\Delta)^{3/2}B_{\rm init}\sigma\tau}{\sqrt{TG}}\right)^{1/3}\right).

(89)

We observe that, in all three cases above, we still have descent with
T
. Therefore, those terms do not contribute to the utility bound. Now we should consider the momentum constraints (a)-(d) that can be combined into one as

\beta\leq\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(\min\left\{\frac{\hat{\beta}\tau}{\sqrt{L\Delta}+B_{\rm init}+\sigma},\frac{1}{\sqrt{c\delta_{\rm byz}}T}\right\}\right).

This translates into the rate of the form

\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(\frac{L\Delta(\sqrt{L\Delta}+B_{\rm init}+\sigma)}{T\hat{\beta}\tau}+L\Delta\sqrt{c\delta_{\rm byz}}\right).

We should plug in the restrictions (
84
) in the rate above. This leads to the rate of the form

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(\frac{L\Delta aC(\sqrt{L\Delta}+B_{\rm init}+\sigma)}{T\sqrt{L\Delta}\tau}+\frac{L\Delta C_{1}a^{2/3}T^{1/3}\tau^{1/3}(\sqrt{L\Delta}+B_{\rm init}+\sigma)}{T\sqrt{L\Delta}\tau}+\frac{L\Delta\tau T(\sqrt{L\Delta}+B_{\rm init}+\sigma)}{T\sqrt{L\Delta}\tau}\right.

\displaystyle\left.+\;L\Delta\sqrt{c\delta_{\rm byz}}\right)

\displaystyle=\;

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(\frac{\sqrt{L\Delta}aC(\sqrt{L\Delta}+B_{\rm init}+\sigma)}{T\tau}+\frac{\sqrt{L\Delta}C_{1}a^{2/3}(\sqrt{L\Delta}+B_{\rm init}+\sigma)}{T^{2/3}\tau^{2/3}}\right.

\displaystyle\left.+\;\sqrt{L\Delta}(\sqrt{L\Delta}+B_{\rm init}+\sigma)((c\delta_{\rm byz})^{1/3}+\sqrt{c\delta_{\rm byz}})\right).

Now we use
a\sim\frac{\sqrt{d}\sigma_{\omega}\sqrt{T}}{\sqrt{G}}
,
C=(1+\sqrt{G})
, and
C_{1}=(1+G^{1/3}(c\delta_{\rm byz})^{1/3})
to obtain the rate

\displaystyle(\sqrt{L\Delta}+B_{\rm init}+\sigma)\cdot\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(\frac{\sqrt{L\Delta}\sqrt{d}\sigma_{\omega}C}{\sqrt{GT}\tau}+\frac{\sqrt{L\Delta}C_{1}d^{1/3}\sigma_{\omega}^{2/3}}{(TG)^{1/3}\tau^{2/3}}+\sqrt{L\Delta}((c\delta_{\rm byz})^{1/3}+\sqrt{c\delta_{\rm byz}})\right)

\displaystyle=\;

\displaystyle(\sqrt{L\Delta}+B_{\rm init}+\sigma)\cdot\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(\frac{\sqrt{L\Delta}\sqrt{d}\sigma_{\omega}(1+\sqrt{c\delta_{\rm byz}G})}{\sqrt{GT}\tau}+\frac{\sqrt{L\Delta}(1+(c\delta_{\rm byz}G)^{1/3})d^{1/3}\sigma_{\omega}^{2/3}}{(TG)^{1/3}\tau^{2/3}}\right.

\displaystyle\left.+\sqrt{L\Delta}((c\delta_{\rm byz})^{1/3}+\sqrt{c\delta_{\rm byz}})\right)

\displaystyle=\;

\displaystyle(\sqrt{L\Delta}+B_{\rm init}+\sigma)\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(\frac{\sqrt{L\Delta d}\sigma_{\omega}}{\sqrt{GT}\tau}+\frac{\sqrt{L\Delta}d^{1/3}\sigma_{\omega}^{2/3}}{(TG)^{1/3}\tau^{2/3}}+\frac{\sqrt{c\delta_{\rm byz}L\Delta d}\sigma_{\omega}}{\sqrt{T}\tau}+\frac{\sqrt{L\Delta}(c\delta_{\rm byz}d\sigma_{\omega}^{2})^{1/3}}{T^{1/3}\tau^{2/3}}\right.

\displaystyle\left.+\sqrt{L\Delta}((c\delta_{\rm byz})^{1/3}+\sqrt{c\delta_{\rm byz}})\right).

We can combine the terms above in a simpler way as follows

\displaystyle(\sqrt{L\Delta}+B_{\rm init}+\sigma)\sqrt{L\Delta}\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(\left(\frac{\sqrt{d}\sigma_{\omega}}{\sqrt{GT}\tau}+\frac{\sqrt{c\delta_{\rm byz}d}\sigma_{\omega}}{\sqrt{T}\tau}+\sqrt{c\delta_{\rm byz}}\right)+\left(\frac{\sqrt{d}\sigma_{\omega}}{\sqrt{GT}\tau}+\frac{\sqrt{c\delta_{\rm byz}d}\sigma_{\omega}}{\sqrt{T}\tau}+\sqrt{c\delta_{\rm byz}}\right)^{2/3}\right).

We observe that the first term is purely due to DP noise, the middle term is a mix of DP noise and robust aggregation, and the last term is purely due to robust aggregation.

Finally, the restriction (
68
) translate to

\displaystyle\gamma\leq\frac{1}{L}\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(\frac{\hat{\beta}\tau}{LB_{\rm init}},\frac{\sqrt{\hat{\beta}\tau}}{L\sqrt{B_{\rm init}}}\right).

(90)

Note that the second restriction in (
90
) does not contribute to the neighborhood. The first restriction in (
90
) is already taken into account by the restriction
\beta\leq\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(\frac{\hat{\beta}\tau}{\sqrt{L\Delta}+B_{\rm init}+\sigma}\right)
.

Case
\mathcal{I}_{K+1}=0.

This case is even easier. The only change will be with the term next to
R^{t}
. We will get

1-\frac{96L^{2}}{\hat{\beta}^{2}\eta^{2}}\gamma^{2}-\frac{24L^{2}}{\beta^{2}}\gamma^{2}\geq\frac{1}{3}-\frac{96L^{2}}{\hat{\beta}^{2}\eta^{2}}\gamma^{2}\geq 0

instead of

1-\frac{32\beta^{2}L^{2}}{\hat{\beta}^{2}\eta^{2}}\gamma^{2}-\frac{96L^{2}}{\hat{\beta}^{2}\eta^{2}}\gamma^{2}-\frac{24L^{2}}{\beta^{2}}\gamma^{2}\geq 0

as in the previous case. This difference comes from
Lemma

11
because
\widetilde{V}^{K+1}=0
. The rest is a repetition of the previous derivations.

Bounds on
\Delta
,
B_{\rm init}
, and
\Delta_{0}
.

It remains to give explicit bounds on
\Delta
and
B_{\rm init}
using problem-dependent constants. With the initialization
v_{i}^{0}=g_{i}^{0}=0
for all
i\in\mathcal{G}
we have that

\displaystyle\Phi^{0}

\displaystyle=f(x^{0})-f^{\star}+\frac{8\gamma\beta}{\hat{\beta}^{2}\eta^{2}}\frac{1}{G}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{G}}\|\nabla f_{i}(x^{0})\|^{2}+\frac{2\gamma}{\beta}\|\nabla f(x^{0})\|^{2}

\displaystyle\overset{(i)}{\leq}f(x^{0})-f^{\star}+\frac{8\gamma\beta}{\hat{\beta}^{2}\eta^{2}}B_{\zeta}\|\nabla f(x^{0})\|^{2}+\frac{8\gamma\beta}{\hat{\beta}^{2}\eta^{2}}\zeta^{2}+\frac{4\gamma}{\beta}L(f(x^{0})-f^{\star})

\displaystyle\overset{(ii)}{\leq}\left(1+\frac{16L\gamma\beta}{\hat{\beta}^{2}\eta^{2}}B_{\zeta}+\frac{4L\gamma}{\beta}\right)(f(x^{0})-f^{\star})+\frac{8\gamma\beta}{\hat{\beta}^{2}\eta^{2}}\zeta^{2}.

(91)

where
(i)
follow from
3.3a
and
L
-smoothness,
(ii)
– from
L
-smoothness. Note that we choose
\beta=12L\gamma
, which implies that
\frac{4L\gamma}{\beta}=\frac{1}{3}
, and from (
68
) we have

\frac{8\gamma\beta}{\hat{\beta}^{2}\eta^{2}}=\frac{96L\gamma^{2}}{\hat{\beta}^{2}\eta^{2}}\leq 96L\cdot\frac{5}{6\cdot 96L^{2}}=\frac{5}{6L}.

Thus, we have the bound on
\Phi^{0}
from (
91
)

\Phi^{0}\leq\left(\nicefrac{{4}}{{3}}+\frac{5}{3}B_{\zeta}\right)(f(x^{0})-f^{\star})+\frac{5}{6L}\zeta^{2}.

Therefore, we have that

L\Delta=\mathcal{O}\left((1+B_{\zeta})L(f(x^{0})-f^{\star})+\zeta^{2}\right)=\mathcal{O}\left((1+B_{\zeta})LF^{0}+\zeta^{2}\right)

Next, we bound
B_{\rm init}=\max\{3\tau,\max_{i\in\mathcal{G}}\|\nabla f_{i}(x^{0})\|+b\}>\tau
. Note that in the theorem, we assume that
\max_{i\in\mathcal{G}}\|\nabla f_{i}(x^{0})\|\geq 3\tau
. This implies that
B_{\rm init}=\max_{i\in\mathcal{G}}\|\nabla f_{i}(x^{0})\|+b
. Therefore, we provide the bound with high probability as follows

\displaystyle B_{\rm init}

\displaystyle=b+\max_{i\in\mathcal{G}}\|\nabla f_{i}(x^{0})\|

\displaystyle=\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(\sigma+\max_{i\in\mathcal{G}}\|\nabla f_{i}(x^{0})-\nabla f(x^{0})\|+\|\nabla f(x^{0})\|\right)

\displaystyle=\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(\sigma+\sqrt{B_{\zeta}LF^{0}}+\zeta+\sqrt{LF^{0}}\right).

where we again use
3.3a
and
L
-smoothness. This allows to bound
\Delta_{0}
as follows

\displaystyle\Delta_{0}

\displaystyle=\sqrt{L\Delta}\left(\sqrt{L\Delta}+B_{\rm init}+\sigma\right)

\displaystyle=\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\left((1+\sqrt{B_{\zeta}})(\sqrt{LF^{0}}+\zeta)(\sigma+(1+\sqrt{B_{\zeta}})\sqrt{LF^{0}}+\zeta)\right)

\displaystyle=\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\left((1+B_{\zeta})LF^{0}+\zeta^{2}+\sigma((1+\sqrt{B_{\zeta}})\sqrt{LF^{0}}+\zeta)\right).

∎

Theorem D.2

(Theorem 3.22 in
[
28
]
)
.

Let
\varepsilon\in(0,1)
be arbitrary. For
c^{2}>2\log(1.25/\delta)
, the Gaussian mechanism with parameter
\sigma_{\omega}\geq c\Delta_{2}(f)/\varepsilon
is
(\varepsilon,\delta)
-differentially private.

Corollary D.1
.

Under the setup of
Theorem

5.1
(
Theorem

D.1
), if we set
\sigma_{\omega}=\Theta\left(\frac{\tau}{\varepsilon}\sqrt{T\log(\frac{T}{\delta})\log(\frac{1}{\delta})}\right)
for some
\varepsilon,\delta\in(0,1)
, then each iteration of
Byz-Clip21-SGD2M
satisfies local
(\varepsilon,\delta)
-DP.

Proof.

We need to plug in the choice of
\sigma_{\omega}
to
Theorem

D.1
and omit all terms that decay with
T
. Note that each step of
Byz-Clip21-SGD2M
satisfies
(\widetilde{\varepsilon},\widetilde{\delta})
-local DP where

\widetilde{\varepsilon}=\frac{\varepsilon}{2\sqrt{2T\log\frac{1}{\delta}}},\quad\widetilde{\delta}=\frac{\delta}{T},

by
Theorem

D.2
. Combining the above result with advanced composition theorem (
[
28
, Theorem 3.20]
), we obtain that all
T
iterations of
Byz-Clip21-SGD2M
satisfy local
(\varepsilon,\delta)
-differentially private.
∎

Appendix E
Proofs in Special Cases

E.1
Convergence in the Absence of Both DP and Byzantine Adversaries

Corollary E.1

(Full statement of
Corollary

5.2
)
.

Let Assumptions
3.1
,
3.2
, and
3.3a
and define
B_{\rm init}\coloneqq\max\{3\tau,\max_{i}\{\|\nabla f_{i}(x^{0})\|\}+b\}
. Let the failure probability
\alpha
be such that
\alpha\in(0,1)
, and constants
b
and
\hat{c}
be defined as in (
67
), and
\Delta\geq\Phi^{0}
for
\Phi^{0}
defined in (
9
). Consider the run of
Byz-Clip21-SGD2M
(
Algorithm

1
) for
T
iterations with no DP and Byzantine adversaries, i.e.,
\sigma_{\omega}=0
and
\delta_{\rm byz}=0
.
Assume the following inequalities hold

1.

step-size restrictions:

(a)

\gamma\leq\frac{1}{24L}
;

(b)

12L\gamma=\beta
;

(c)

\frac{5}{6}-\frac{32L^{2}\beta^{2}}{\hat{\beta}^{2}\eta^{2}}\gamma^{2}-\frac{96L^{2}}{\hat{\beta}^{2}\eta^{2}}\gamma^{2}\geq 0.

(92)

2.

momentum restrictions:

(a)

\hat{\beta}=1;

(b)

\beta\leq\min\left\{\frac{8\tau}{431\sqrt{L\Delta}},1\right\};

(c)

\beta\leq\min\left\{\frac{2\tau}{39(B_{\rm init}-\tau)},1\right\};

(d)

\beta\leq\min\left\{\frac{2\tau}{69b},1\right\};

(e)

and momentum restrictions defined in (
71
), (
72
), (
D
), (
D
), (
75
), (
77
), (
76
), (
78
), (
79
), and (
80
) with
\hat{\beta}=1
,
a,\hat{a}=0,\delta_{\rm byz}=0
;

Then, with probability
1-\alpha
, we bound
\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=0}^{T-1}\|\nabla f(x^{t})\|^{2}
with

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(\frac{L\Delta(1+\nicefrac{{B_{\rm init}}}{{\tau}})}{T}+\frac{\sigma(\sqrt{L\Delta}+B_{\rm init}+\sigma)}{\sqrt{TG}}\right),

where
\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}
hides constant and logarithmic factors and higher order terms decreasing in
T
.

Proof.

In the case, when
\hat{\beta}=1
the worst dependency in (
D
) w.r.t.
T
comes from the terms
①
,
\text{③},

\text{⑤},
and
⑥
, and equals
\gamma\sim\frac{1}{T^{1/2}}
. We also have restriction
\gamma\leq\mathcal{O}(\nicefrac{{1}}{{L}})
and (
90
). All of those restrictions give the rate of the form

\displaystyle\frac{L\Delta}{T}\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(1+\frac{B_{\rm init}}{\tau}+\frac{\sqrt{B_{\rm init}}}{\sqrt{\tau}}+\left(\frac{T\sigma^{2}}{L\Delta G}\right)^{1/2}+\frac{\sigma(\sqrt{L\Delta}+B_{\rm init}+\sigma)T^{1/2}}{L\Delta\sqrt{G}}\right)

\displaystyle=\;

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(\frac{L\Delta(1+\nicefrac{{B_{\rm init}}}{{\tau}})}{T}+\frac{\sigma\sqrt{L\Delta}}{\sqrt{TG}}+\frac{\sigma(\sqrt{L\Delta}+B_{\rm init}+\sigma)}{\sqrt{TG}}\right)

\displaystyle=\;

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(\frac{L\Delta(1+\nicefrac{{B_{\rm init}}}{{\tau}})}{T}+\frac{\sigma(\sqrt{L\Delta}+B_{\rm init}+\sigma)}{\sqrt{TG}}\right),

(93)

where we hide all logarithmic factors in the
\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}
notation.
∎

E.3
Convergence in the Absence of DP Adversaries

In this setting, server-side momentum and clipping are unnecessary. We thus focus on the specialization of
Byz-Clip21-SGD2M
with
\hat{\beta}=1
and
\tau=+\infty
, shown in
Algorithm

2
. Under these settings, the method reduces to the algorithm of
56
. In particular, the error-feedback mechanism is disabled, so the server does not store
g_{i}^{t}
. The analysis correspondingly simplifies and only requires a simpler Lyapunov function

\widetilde{\Phi}^{t}\coloneqq\delta^{t}+\frac{\gamma}{\beta}\|\overline{v}^{t}-\nabla f(x^{t})\|^{2}.

(95)

Also, constants
b
and
c
from (
67
) should be adjusted accordingly. We make use of their replacements
\widetilde{b}
and
\widetilde{c}
defined as

\displaystyle\tilde{b}^{2}\coloneqq 2\sigma^{2}\log\left(\frac{8(T+1)G}{\alpha}\right),\quad\tilde{c}^{2}\coloneqq\left(\sqrt{2}+2\sqrt{3\log\frac{8(T+1)}{\alpha}}\right)^{2}\sigma^{2},

\displaystyle\tilde{z}^{2}\coloneqq\left(\sqrt{2}+2\sqrt{3\log\frac{8G(T+1)}{\alpha}}\right)^{2}\sigma^{2}.

(96)

Algorithm 2

Byz-Clip21-SGD2M
without DP adversaries

1:

Input:

x^{0}\in X,
momentum parameter
\beta\in(0,1],
step-size
\gamma>0

2:

for

t=0,\ldots,T-1

do

3:

x^{t+1}=x^{t}-\gamma g^{t}

4:

for

i\in\mathcal{G}

do

5:

v_{i}^{t+1}=(1-\beta)v_{i}^{t}+\beta\nabla f_{i}(x^{t+1},\xi^{t+1}_{i})

6:

end

for

7:

for

i\in\mathcal{B}

do

8:

v_{i}^{t+1}=(*)

sends arbitrary vector

9:

end

for

10:

g^{t+1}={\rm RAgg}(v_{1}^{t+1},\dots,v_{n}^{t+1})

11:

end

for

E.3.1
Useful Lemmas

We will need to re-derive several descent lemmas as well as introduce new ones.

Lemma 12
.

Let
\{v_{i}^{t}\}
be generated by
Algorithm

2
. Then we have with probability at least
1-\frac{\alpha}{8(T+1)}

\frac{1}{G}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{G}}\|\overline{v}^{t}-v_{i}^{t}\|^{2}\leq 2\zeta^{2}+2\beta\tilde{z}^{2}.

Proof.

We have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{G}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{G}}\left\|v_{i}^{t}-\overline{v}^{t}\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle\overset{(i)}{=}\frac{1}{G}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{G}}\|(1-\beta)(v_{i}^{t-1}-\overline{v}^{t-1})+\beta(\nabla f_{i}(x^{t})-\nabla f(x^{t}))+\beta(\theta_{i}^{t}-\theta^{t})\|^{2}

\displaystyle\overset{(ii)}{=}\frac{1}{G}\left\|\beta\sum_{k=0}^{t}(1-\beta)^{t-k}(\nabla f_{i}(x^{k})-\nabla f(x^{k}))+\beta\sum_{k=0}^{t}(1-\beta)^{t-k}(\theta_{i}^{k}-\theta^{k})\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle\overset{(iii)}{\leq}\frac{2}{G}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{G}}\left\|\beta\sum_{k=0}^{t}(1-\beta)^{t-k}(\nabla f_{i}(x^{k})-\nabla f(x^{k}))\right\|^{2}+\frac{2\beta^{2}}{G}\left\|\sum_{k=0}^{t}(1-\beta)^{t-k}(\theta_{i}^{k}-\theta^{k})\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle\overset{(iv)}{\leq}\frac{2(1-(1-\beta)^{t+1})}{G}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{G}}\sum_{k=0}^{t}\beta(1-\beta)^{t-k}\|\nabla f_{i}(x^{k})-\nabla f(x^{k})\|^{2}

\displaystyle\hskip 199.16928pt+\frac{2\beta^{2}}{G}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{G}}\left\|\sum_{k=0}^{t}(1-\beta)^{t-k}(\theta_{i}^{k}-\theta^{k})\right\|^{2},

\displaystyle\overset{(v)}{\leq}2\sum_{k=0}^{t}\beta(1-\beta)^{t-k}\zeta^{2}+\frac{2\beta^{2}}{G}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{G}}\left\|\sum_{k=0}^{t}(1-\beta)^{t-k}(\theta_{i}^{k}-\theta^{k})\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 2\zeta^{2}+\frac{2\beta^{2}}{G}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{G}}\left\|\sum_{k=0}^{t}(1-\beta)^{t-k}(\theta_{i}^{k}-\theta^{k})\right\|^{2},

(97)

where
(i)
– from the update rules of
v_{i}^{t}
and decomposition
\nabla f_{i}(x^{t})+\theta_{i}^{t}=\nabla f_{i}(x^{t},\xi_{i}^{t})
,
(ii)
– unrolling the recursion till the zero iteration and using the initialization with zeros,
(iii)
– from Young’s inequality,
(iv)
– from Jensen’s inequality,
(v)
– from
3.3a
. For the second term in (
97
), we use
Lemma

2
. We know that
\theta_{i}^{k}-\theta^{k}
is a zero-centered sub-Gaussian r.v. conditioned on all events before iteration
k
. Therefore, we have

\left\|\sum_{k=0}^{t}(1-\beta)^{t-k}(\theta_{i}^{k}-\theta^{k})\right\|^{2}\leq(\sqrt{2}+2u)\sqrt{\sum_{k=0}^{t}(1-\beta)^{2(t-k)}\sigma^{2}}\leq(\sqrt{2}+2u)\frac{\sigma}{\sqrt{\beta}}\leq\frac{\tilde{z}}{\sqrt{\beta}},

(98)

where
u=\sqrt{3\log\frac{8G(T+1)}{\alpha}}.
Thus, we obtain with probability at least
1-G\cdot\frac{\alpha}{8G(T+1)}
that

\frac{1}{G}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{G}}\|v_{i}^{t}-\overline{v}^{t}\|^{2}\leq 2\zeta^{2}+2\beta\tilde{z}^{2}.

(99)

∎

Next, we re-derive the descent lemma for the function value.

Lemma 13
.

Let
f
be
L
-smooth, Assumption
3.3a
hold with
A=0
,
F^{t}\coloneqq f(x^{t})-f^{\star},

\{x^{t}\}
be generated by
Algorithm

2
. Assume that

1.

\gamma\leq\frac{1}{2L}
;

2.

\|\theta_{i}^{t}\|\leq\widetilde{b}
;

3.

\frac{1}{G}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{G}}\|\overline{v}^{t-1}-v_{i}^{t-1}\|^{2}\leq 2\zeta^{2}+2\beta\tilde{z}^{2}
;

Then we have

\displaystyle f(x^{t+1})

\displaystyle\leq f(x^{t})-\frac{\gamma}{2}\|\nabla f(x^{t})\|^{2}-\frac{1}{4\gamma}\|x^{t+1}-x^{t}\|^{2}+\gamma\|\nabla f(x^{t})-\overline{v}^{t}\|^{2}+2c\delta_{\rm byz}\zeta^{2}+2c\delta_{\rm byz}\beta\tilde{z}^{2}.

(100)

Proof.

Using the derivations from
[
54
]
, Lemma 2 we first get

\displaystyle f(x^{t+1})

\displaystyle\leq f(x^{t})-\frac{\gamma}{2}\|\nabla f(x^{t})\|^{2}-\frac{\gamma}{4}\|g^{t}\|^{2}+\frac{\gamma}{2}\|\nabla f(x^{t})-g^{t}\|^{2}.

We continue the derivations as follows

\displaystyle f(x^{t+1})

\displaystyle\overset{(i)}{\leq}f(x^{t})-\frac{\gamma}{2}\|\nabla f(x^{t})\|^{2}-\frac{\gamma}{4}\|g^{t}\|^{2}+\gamma\|\nabla f(x^{t})-\overline{v}^{t}\|^{2}+\gamma\|\overline{v}^{t}-g^{t}\|^{2}

\displaystyle\overset{(ii)}{=}f(x^{t})-\frac{\gamma}{2}\|\nabla f(x^{t})\|^{2}-\frac{1}{4\gamma}\|x^{t+1}-x^{t}\|^{2}+\gamma\|\nabla f(x^{t})-\overline{v}^{t}\|^{2}+\gamma\|\overline{v}^{t}-g^{t}\|^{2},

(101)

where
(i)
follows from Jensen’s inequality applied to
\|\cdot\|^{2}
,
(ii)
— from the update rule of
x^{t}
. We bound the term
\|\overline{v}^{t}-g^{t}\|^{2}
using properties of the aggregator.

\displaystyle\|\overline{v}^{t}-g^{t}\|^{2}

\displaystyle=\|\overline{v}^{t}-{\rm RAgg}(v_{1}^{t},\dots,v_{n}^{t})\|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{c\delta_{\rm byz}}{G}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{G}}\|v_{i}^{t}-\overline{v}^{t}\|^{2}

\displaystyle\overset{(iii)}{\leq}2c\delta_{\rm byz}\zeta^{2}+2c\delta_{\rm byz}\beta\tilde{z}^{2}.

(102)

where
(iii)
holds by
Lemma

12
. Plugging in (
E.3.1
) into (
E.3.1
), we obtain the statement of the lemma.

∎

Lemma 14
.

Let each
f_{i}
be
L
-smooth, and
\widetilde{\Delta}\geq\Phi^{0}.
Assume that the following inequalities hold for the iterates generated by
Byz-Clip21-SGD2M

1.

\gamma\leq\frac{1}{6L}
,

2.

\beta\in[0,1]
,

3.

\|\overline{v}^{t-1}\|\leq\sqrt{64L\widetilde{\Delta}}+3\frac{\widetilde{c}}{\sqrt{G}}+3\sqrt{2c\delta_{\rm byz}}\zeta+3\sqrt{\beta}\sqrt{2c\delta_{\rm byz}}\tilde{z}
,

4.

\|\nabla f(x^{t-1})-\overline{v}^{t-1}\|\leq\sqrt{4L\widetilde{\Delta}}+\frac{3}{2}\frac{\widetilde{c}}{\sqrt{G}}+\frac{3}{2}\sqrt{2c\delta_{\rm byz}}\zeta+\frac{3}{2}\sqrt{\beta}\sqrt{2c\delta_{\rm byz}}\tilde{z}
,

5.

\|\theta^{t}\|\leq\frac{\widetilde{c}}{\sqrt{G}}
,

6.

\frac{1}{G}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{G}}\|\overline{v}^{t-1}-v_{i}^{t-1}\|^{2}\leq 2c\delta_{\rm byz}\zeta^{2}+2c\delta_{\rm byz}\beta\tilde{z}^{2}
,

7.

\Phi^{t-1}\leq 2\widetilde{\Delta}.

Then we have

\displaystyle\|\overline{v}^{t}\|\leq\sqrt{64L\widetilde{\Delta}}+3\frac{\widetilde{c}}{\sqrt{G}}+3\sqrt{2c\delta_{\rm byz}}\zeta+3\sqrt{2c\delta_{\rm byz}}\sqrt{\beta}\tilde{z}.

(103)

Proof.

We start as follows

\displaystyle\|\overline{v}^{t}\|

\displaystyle\overset{(i)}{=}\|(1-\beta)\overline{v}^{t-1}+\beta\nabla f(x^{t},\xi^{t})\|

\displaystyle=\|(1-\beta)(\overline{v}^{t-1}-\nabla f(x^{t-1}))+\beta(\nabla f(x^{t})-\nabla f(x^{t-1}))+\beta(\nabla f(x^{t},\xi^{t})-\nabla f(x^{t}))+\nabla f(x^{t-1})\|

\displaystyle\overset{(ii)}{\leq}(1-\beta)\|\overline{v}^{t-1}-\nabla f(x^{t-1})\|+\beta L\gamma\|g^{t-1}\|+\beta\|\theta^{t}\|+\|\nabla f(x^{t-1})\|.

(104)

where
(i)
follows from the update rule of
\overline{v}^{t}
,
(ii)
— from triangle inequality and
L
-smoothness. We continue the derivations as follows

\displaystyle\|\overline{v}^{t}\|

\displaystyle\overset{(iii)}{\leq}\sqrt{2L(f(x^{t-1})-f^{\star})}+(1-\beta)\|\overline{v}^{t-1}-\nabla f(x^{t-1})\|+\beta\frac{\widetilde{c}}{\sqrt{G}}+\beta L\gamma\|\overline{v}^{t-1}\|+\beta L\gamma\|\overline{v}^{t-1}-g^{t-1}\|.

(105)

where
(iii)
follows from
L
-smoothness, assumption 5 of the lemma, and triangle inequality. The term
\|\overline{v}^{t-1}-g^{t-1}\|
can be bounded by
\sqrt{2c\delta_{\rm byz}\zeta^{2}+2c\delta_{\rm byz}\beta\tilde{c}^{2}}
by (
E.3.1
) and assumptions 5, 6 of the lemma. We continue further bounding as follows

\displaystyle\|\overline{v}^{t}\|

\displaystyle\overset{(iv)}{\leq}\sqrt{4L\widetilde{\Delta}}+(1-\beta)\left(\sqrt{4L\widetilde{\Delta}}+\frac{3}{2}\frac{\widetilde{c}}{\sqrt{G}}+\frac{3}{2}\sqrt{2c\delta_{\rm byz}}\zeta+\frac{3}{2}\sqrt{2c\delta_{\rm byz}}\sqrt{\beta}\tilde{z}\right)+\beta\frac{\widetilde{c}}{\sqrt{G}}

\displaystyle\quad+L\gamma\beta\left(\sqrt{64L\widetilde{\Delta}}+3\frac{\widetilde{c}}{\sqrt{G}}+3\sqrt{2c\delta_{\rm byz}}\zeta+3\sqrt{2c\delta_{\rm byz}}\sqrt{\beta}\tilde{z}\right)+\beta L\gamma\sqrt{2c\delta_{\rm byz}}\zeta+\beta L\gamma\sqrt{2c\delta_{\rm byz}}\sqrt{\beta}\tilde{z}

\displaystyle=\sqrt{L\widetilde{\Delta}}(2+2(1-\beta)+8L\gamma\beta)+\frac{\widetilde{c}}{\sqrt{G}}(\nicefrac{{3}}{{2}}(1-\beta)+\beta+3L\gamma\beta)+\sqrt{2c\delta_{\rm byz}}\zeta(\nicefrac{{3}}{{2}}(1-\beta)+3L\gamma\beta+\beta L\gamma)

\displaystyle\quad+\sqrt{2c\delta_{\rm byz}}\sqrt{\beta}\tilde{z}(\nicefrac{{3}}{{2}}(1-\beta)+3L\gamma\beta+\beta L\gamma).

(106)

For the first coefficient preceding
\widetilde{\Delta}
, we have

2+2(1-\beta)+8L\gamma\beta\leq 8\Leftarrow 1-\beta+4L\gamma\beta\leq 3\leq L\gamma\beta\leq 1,

where the last inequality holds by the choice of
\beta
and
\gamma
. For the second coefficient preceding
\frac{\widetilde{c}}{\sqrt{G}}
, we have

\frac{3}{2}(1-\beta)+\beta+3L\gamma\beta\leq 3\Leftarrow 4L\gamma\beta\leq\frac{3}{2}(1+\beta),

where the last inequality holds by the choice of
\beta
and
\gamma
. For the third coefficient preceding
\sqrt{2c\delta_{\rm byz}}\zeta
, we have

\frac{3}{2}(1-\beta)+3L\gamma\beta+\beta L\gamma\leq 3\Leftarrow 3L\gamma\beta\leq 1,

where the last inequality holds by the choice of
\beta
and
\gamma
. For the fourth coefficient preceding
\sqrt{2c\delta_{\rm byz}}\sqrt{\beta}\tilde{z}
, we have

\frac{3}{2}(1-\beta)+3L\gamma\beta+\beta L\gamma\leq 3\Leftarrow 4L\gamma\beta\leq\frac{3}{2}(1+\beta),

where the last inequality holds by the choice of
\beta
and
\gamma
.
∎

Lemma 15
.

Let each
f_{i}
be
L
-smooth,
\widetilde{\Delta}\geq\Phi^{0}
. Assume that the following inequalities hold for the iterates generated by
Byz-Clip21-SGD2M

1.

\gamma\leq\frac{1}{6L}
;

2.

4L\gamma\leq\beta
;

3.

\beta\in[0,1];

4.

\frac{1}{G}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{G}}\|v_{i}^{t-1}-\overline{v}^{t-1}\|^{2}\leq 2c\delta_{\rm byz}\zeta^{2}+2c\delta_{\rm byz}\beta\tilde{z}^{2};

5.

\|\overline{v}^{t-1}\|\leq\sqrt{64L\widetilde{\Delta}}+3\frac{\widetilde{c}}{\sqrt{G}}+3\sqrt{2c\delta_{\rm byz}}\zeta+3\sqrt{2c\delta_{\rm byz}}\sqrt{\beta}\tilde{z}
,

6.

\|\nabla f(x^{t-1})-\overline{v}^{t-1}\|\leq\sqrt{4L\widetilde{\Delta}}+\frac{3}{2}\frac{\widetilde{c}}{\sqrt{G}}+\frac{3}{2}\sqrt{2c\delta_{\rm byz}}\zeta+\frac{3}{2}\sqrt{2c\delta_{\rm byz}}\sqrt{\beta}\tilde{z}
;

7.

\|\theta^{t}\|\leq\frac{\widetilde{c}}{\sqrt{G}}
;

8.

\|\theta_{i}^{t}\|\leq\widetilde{b}
for all
i\in\mathcal{G};

9.

\Phi^{t-1}\leq 2\widetilde{\Delta}.

Then we have

\displaystyle\|\nabla f(x^{t})-\overline{v}^{t}\|\leq\sqrt{4L\widetilde{\Delta}}+\frac{3}{2}\frac{\widetilde{c}}{\sqrt{G}}+\frac{3}{2}\sqrt{2c\delta_{\rm byz}}\zeta+\frac{3}{2}\sqrt{2c\delta_{\rm byz}}\sqrt{\beta}\tilde{z}.

(107)

Proof.

We have

\displaystyle\|\nabla f(x^{t})-v^{t}\|

\displaystyle\overset{(i)}{=}\|\nabla f(x^{t})-(1-\beta)\overline{v}^{t-1}-\beta\nabla f_{i}(x^{t},\xi^{t})\|

\displaystyle\overset{(ii)}{\leq}(1-\beta)\|\nabla f(x^{t})-\overline{v}^{t-1}\|+\beta\|\nabla f(x^{t})-\nabla f(x^{t},\xi^{t})\|

\displaystyle\overset{(iii)}{\leq}(1-\beta)\|\nabla f(x^{t})-\nabla f(x^{t-1})\|+(1-\beta)\|\nabla f(x^{t-1})-\overline{v}^{t-1}\|+\beta\|\theta^{t}\|

\displaystyle\overset{(iv)}{\leq}(1-\beta)L\gamma\|g^{t-1}\|+(1-\beta)\|\nabla f(x^{t-1})-\overline{v}^{t-1}\|+\beta\|\theta^{t}\|

\displaystyle\overset{(v)}{\leq}(1-\beta)L\gamma\|\overline{v}^{t-1}-g^{t-1}\|+(1-\beta)L\gamma\|\overline{v}^{t-1}\|+(1-\beta)\|\nabla f(x^{t-1})-\overline{v}^{t-1}\|+\beta\|\theta^{t}\|,

(108)

where
(i)
follows from the update rule of
v^{t}
,
(ii)
-
(iii)
— from the triangle inequality,
(iv)
— from the update rule of
x^{t}
and
L
-smoothness,
(v)
— from triangle inequality. We continue as follows

\displaystyle\|\nabla f(x^{t})-\overline{v}^{t}\|

\displaystyle\overset{(vi)}{\leq}\sqrt{2c\delta_{\rm byz}}(1-\beta)L\gamma\zeta+\sqrt{2c\delta_{\rm byz}}(1-\beta)L\gamma\sqrt{\beta}\tilde{z}+(1-\beta)L\gamma\|\overline{v}^{t-1}\|+\beta\|\theta^{t}\|

\displaystyle\qquad+\;(1-\beta)\|\nabla f(x^{t-1})-\overline{v}^{t-1}\|

\displaystyle\overset{(vii)}{\leq}\sqrt{2c\delta_{\rm byz}}(1-\beta)L\gamma\zeta+\sqrt{2c\delta_{\rm byz}}(1-\beta)L\gamma\sqrt{\beta}\tilde{z}

\displaystyle\quad+(1-\beta)L\gamma\left(\sqrt{64L\widetilde{\Delta}}+3\frac{\widetilde{c}}{\sqrt{G}}+3\sqrt{2c\delta_{\rm byz}}\zeta+3\sqrt{2c\delta_{\rm byz}}\sqrt{\beta}\tilde{z}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\beta\frac{\widetilde{c}}{\sqrt{G}}+(1-\beta)\left(\sqrt{4L\widetilde{\Delta}}+\frac{3}{2}\frac{\widetilde{c}}{\sqrt{G}}+\frac{3}{2}\sqrt{2c\delta_{\rm byz}}\zeta+\frac{3}{2}\sqrt{2c\delta_{\rm byz}}\sqrt{\beta}\tilde{z}\right)

\displaystyle=\sqrt{L\widetilde{\Delta}}(8(1-\beta)L\gamma+2(1-\beta))+\frac{\widetilde{c}}{\sqrt{G}}(3(1-\beta)L\gamma+\nicefrac{{3}}{{2}}(1-\beta))

\displaystyle\quad+\sqrt{2c\delta_{\rm byz}}\zeta((1-\beta)L\gamma+3(1-\beta)L\gamma+\nicefrac{{3}}{{2}}(1-\beta))

\displaystyle\quad+\sqrt{2c\delta_{\rm byz}}\sqrt{\beta}\tilde{z}((1-\beta)L\gamma+3(1-\beta)L\gamma+\nicefrac{{3}}{{2}}(1-\beta)).

where
(vi)
follows from (
E.3.1
) and assumptions 4, 8 of the lemma,
(vii)
— from assumptions 5, 6, 7 of the lemma.

For the first coefficient preceding
\sqrt{L\widetilde{\Delta}}
, we have

8(1-\beta)L\gamma+2(1-\beta)\leq 2\Leftarrow 4L\gamma\leq\beta,

where the last inequality holds by assumption 2 of the lemma. For the second coefficient preceding
\frac{\widetilde{c}}{\sqrt{G}}
, we have

3(1-\beta)L\gamma+\frac{3}{2}(1-\beta)\leq\frac{3}{2}\Leftarrow 2L\gamma\leq\beta,

where the last inequality holds by assumption 2 of the lemma. For the third coefficient preceding
\sqrt{2c\delta_{\rm byz}}\zeta
, we have

4(1-\beta)L\gamma+\frac{3}{2}(1-\beta)\leq\frac{3}{2}\Leftarrow 4L\gamma\leq\frac{3}{2}\beta\Leftarrow\frac{8}{3}L\gamma\leq\beta,

where the last inequality holds by assumption 2 of the lemma. Finally, or the fourth coefficient preceding
\sqrt{2c\delta_{\rm byz}}\sqrt{\beta}\tilde{z}
, we have

4(1-\beta)L\gamma+\frac{3}{2}(1-\beta)\leq\frac{3}{2}\Leftarrow 4L\gamma\leq\frac{3}{2}\beta\Leftarrow\frac{8}{3}L\gamma\leq\beta,

where the last inequality holds by assumption 2 of the lemma. This finalizes the proof.
∎

E.3.2
Main Convergence Theorem

Theorem E.1

(Full statement of
Theorem

5.2
)
.

Let Assumptions
3.1
,
3.2
, and
3.3
hold. Let the failure probability
\alpha\in(0,1)
, and constants
\widetilde{b}
and
\widetilde{c}
be defined as in (
E.3
), and
\widetilde{\Delta}\geq\widetilde{\Phi}^{0}
for
\widetilde{\Phi}^{0}
defined in (
95
). Consider the run of
Byz-Clip21-SGD2M
with
\sigma_{\omega}=0
and
\tau=+\infty
(
Algorithm

2
) for T iterations. Assume that the following inequalities are satisfied

1.

\gamma\leq\frac{1}{6L}
,

2.

\beta\geq 4L\gamma
,

3.

and momentum restrictions defined in (
112
), (
113
), and (
114
).

Then, with probability at least
1-\alpha
, we bound
\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=0}^{T-1}\|\nabla f(x^{t})\|^{2}
with

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(\frac{L\widetilde{\Delta}}{T}+\left(\frac{\sigma^{2}L\widetilde{\Delta}}{GT}\right)^{1/2}+\frac{\sigma(\sqrt{L\widetilde{\Delta}}+\sigma/\sqrt{G}+\sqrt{c\delta_{\rm byz}}\zeta+\sqrt{c\delta_{\rm byz}}\sigma)}{\sqrt{GT}}+c\delta_{\rm byz}\zeta^{2}\right),

(109)

where we can choose
\widetilde{\Delta}=2(f(x^{0})-f^{\star})=2F^{0}.

Proof.

For convenience, we define
\nabla f_{i}(x^{-1},\xi^{-1}_{i})=v_{i}^{-1}=g_{i}^{-1}=0,\Phi^{-1}=\Phi^{0}
. Next, let us define an event
\widetilde{E}^{t}
for each
t\in\{0,\dots,T\}
such that the following inequalities hold for all
k\in\{0,\dots,t\}

1.

\frac{1}{G}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{G}}\|\overline{v}^{t}-v_{i}^{t}\|\leq 2\zeta^{2}+2\beta\tilde{z}^{2}
;

2.

\|\theta^{k}_{i}\|\leq b
for all
i\in\mathcal{G}
and
\|\theta^{k}\|\leq\frac{\widetilde{c}}{\sqrt{G}}
;

3.

\|\overline{v}^{t-1}\|\leq\sqrt{64L\widetilde{\Delta}}+3\frac{\widetilde{c}}{\sqrt{G}}+3\sqrt{2c\delta_{\rm byz}}\zeta+3\sqrt{2c\delta_{\rm byz}}\sqrt{\beta}\tilde{z}
,

4.

\|\nabla f(x^{t-1})-\overline{v}^{t-1}\|\leq\sqrt{4L\widetilde{\Delta}}+\frac{3}{2}\frac{\widetilde{c}}{\sqrt{G}}+\frac{3}{2}\sqrt{2c\delta_{\rm byz}}\zeta+\frac{3}{2}\sqrt{2c\delta_{\rm byz}}\sqrt{\beta}\tilde{z}
;

5.

\widetilde{\Phi}^{k}\leq 2\widetilde{\Delta}
;

6.

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\Delta

\displaystyle\geq 2\gamma(1-\beta)\sum_{l=0}^{k-1}\langle\nabla f(x^{l})-\nabla f(x^{l+1}),\theta^{l+1}\rangle.

Then, we will derive the result by induction, i.e., using the induction w.r.t.
t
, we will show that
\mathbb{P}(\widetilde{E}^{t})\geq 1-\frac{\alpha(t+1)}{T+1}
for all
t\in\{0,\dots,T-1\}
.

Before moving on to the proof’s induction part, we need to establish several useful bounds. Denote the events
\widetilde{\Theta}^{t}_{i}
and
\widetilde{\Theta}^{t}
as

\displaystyle\widetilde{\Theta}^{t}_{i}\coloneqq\{\|\theta^{t}_{i}\|\geq\widetilde{b}\},\quad\widetilde{\Theta}^{t}\coloneqq\left\{\|\theta^{t}\|\geq\frac{\widetilde{c}}{\sqrt{G}}\right\},\quad\hat{\Theta}^{t}_{i}\coloneqq\left\{\left\|\sum_{k=0}^{t}(1-\beta)^{t-k}(\theta_{i}^{k}-\theta^{k})\right\|\geq\frac{\tilde{z}}{\sqrt{\beta}}\right\}

(110)

respectively. From
3.2
we have

\mathbb{P}(\widetilde{\Theta}^{t}_{i})\leq 2\exp\left(-\frac{\widetilde{b}^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}}\right)=\frac{\alpha}{8(T+1)G}

where the last equality is by definition of
\widetilde{b}^{2}
. Therefore,
\mathbb{P}(\overline{\widetilde{\Theta}}^{t}_{i})\geq 1-\frac{\alpha}{8G(T+1)}.

Besides, notice that the constant
\widetilde{c}
in (
E.3
) can be viewed as

\widetilde{c}^{2}=(\sqrt{2}+2\widetilde{b}_{3})\sigma^{2}\quad\text{where}\quad\widetilde{b}_{3}^{2}=3\log\frac{8(T+1)}{\alpha}.

Now, we can use
Lemma

2
to bound
\mathbb{P}(\widetilde{\Theta}^{t}).
Since all
\theta^{t}_{i}
are independent
\sigma
-sub-Gaussian random vectors, then we have

\mathbb{P}\left(\left\|\sum_{i\in\mathcal{G}}\theta^{t}_{i}\right\|\geq\widetilde{c}\sqrt{G}\right)=\mathbb{P}\left(\|\theta^{t}\|\geq\frac{\widetilde{c}}{\sqrt{G}}\right)\leq\exp(-\widetilde{b}_{3}^{2}/3)=\frac{\alpha}{8(T+1)}.

The bound
\mathbb{P}(\hat{\Theta}^{t}_{i})\leq\frac{\alpha}{6G(T+1)}
was shown before in
Lemma

12
.

Now, we are ready to prove that
\mathbb{P}(\widetilde{E}^{t})\geq 1-\frac{\alpha(t+1)}{T+1}
for all
t\in\{0,\dots,T-1\}.
First, we show that the base of induction holds.

Base of induction.

1.

To establish the first, we use
Lemma

12
, which guarantees the bound in
\cap_{i\in\mathcal{G}}\overline{\hat{\Theta}^{t}}
, i.e., with probability
1-\frac{\alpha}{8(T+1)}
.

2.

\overline{v}^{0}=\frac{1}{G}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{G}}((1-\beta)v_{i}^{-1}+\beta\nabla f_{i}(x^{0},\xi^{0}_{i}))=\frac{\beta}{G}\nabla f(x^{0},\xi^{0}).
Therefore, we have

\displaystyle\|\overline{g}^{0}\|

\displaystyle=\beta\|\nabla f(x^{0},\xi^{0})\|

\displaystyle\leq\beta\|\nabla f(x^{0})\|+\beta\|\nabla f(x^{0},\xi^{0})-\nabla f(x^{0})\|

\displaystyle\leq\beta\sqrt{2L(f(x^{0})-f^{\star})}+\beta\frac{\widetilde{c}}{\sqrt{G}}

\displaystyle\leq\beta\sqrt{2L\widetilde{\Phi}^{0}}+\beta\frac{\widetilde{c}}{\sqrt{G}}

\displaystyle\leq\beta\sqrt{4L\widetilde{\Delta}}+\beta\frac{\widetilde{c}}{\sqrt{G}}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{64L\widetilde{\Delta}}+\frac{3\widetilde{c}}{\sqrt{G}}+3\sqrt{2c\delta_{\rm byz}}\zeta+3\sqrt{2c\delta_{\rm byz}}\sqrt{\beta}\tilde{z}.

The inequalities above again hold in
\overline{\widetilde{\Theta}^{0}}
, i.e., with probability at least
1-\frac{\alpha}{8(T+1)}.
Therefore, the condition
3
of the induction is verified.

3.

We have

\displaystyle\|\overline{v}^{0}-\nabla f(x^{0})\|

\displaystyle=\|\beta\nabla f(x^{0},\xi^{0})-\nabla f(x^{0})\|

\displaystyle\leq\beta\|\nabla f(x^{0},\xi^{0})-\nabla f(x^{0})\|+(1-\beta)\|\nabla f(x^{0})\|

\displaystyle\leq\beta b+(1-\beta)\sqrt{4L\widetilde{\Delta}}.

The bound above holds with probability at least
1-\frac{\alpha}{8(T+1)}
because it holds in
\cap_{i\in\mathcal{G}}\overline{\widetilde{\Theta}_{i}^{0}}.
Therefore, the bound
4
of the assumption of the induction is verified.

4.

Next, we emphasize that the condition
8
of the induction assumption also hold, as
\widetilde{\Phi}^{0}\leq 2\widetilde{\Phi}^{0}\leq 2\widetilde{\Delta}
by the choice of
\widetilde{\Delta}
.

5.

We finalize the induction base by noting that the condition
6
of the induction assumption holds since the RHS equals
0
.

Therefore, we conclude that the conditions
1
-
8
hold with a probability of at least

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\overline{\widetilde{\Theta}^{0}}\cap\left(\cap_{i\in\mathcal{G}}\overline{\widetilde{\Theta}_{i}^{0}}\right)\cap\left(\cap_{i\in\mathcal{G}}\overline{\hat{\Theta}^{0}_{i}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\geq 1-\mathbb{P}(\widetilde{\Theta}^{0})-\sum_{i\in\mathcal{G}}\mathbb{P}(\widetilde{\Theta}_{i}^{0})-\sum_{i\in\mathcal{G}}\mathbb{P}(\hat{\Theta}_{i}^{0})

\displaystyle\geq 1-\frac{\alpha}{8(T+1)}-G\cdot\frac{\alpha}{8G(T+1)}-G\cdot\frac{\alpha}{8G(T+1)}

\displaystyle=1-\frac{\alpha}{2(T+1)}>1-\frac{\alpha}{T+1},

i.e.,
\mathbb{P}(E^{0})\geq 1-\frac{\alpha}{T+1}
holds. This is the base of the induction.

Transition step of induction.

Assume that all events
\overline{\widetilde{\Theta}^{K+1}},\overline{\widetilde{\Theta}^{K+1}_{i}}
take place, i.e.,
\|\theta^{K+1}_{i}\|\leq\widetilde{b},\|\theta^{K+1}\|\leq\frac{\widetilde{c}}{\sqrt{G}}
for all
i\in\mathcal{G}
. That is, we assume that the event

\overline{\widetilde{\Theta}^{K+1}}\cap\left(\cap_{i\in\mathcal{G}}\cap\overline{\widetilde{\Theta}^{K+1}_{i}}\right)\cap\left(\cap_{i\in\mathcal{G}}\overline{\hat{\Theta}_{i}^{K+1}}\right)\cap\widetilde{E}^{K}

holds. Then, by the assumptions of the induction and from
Lemma

14
, we get that

\|\overline{v}^{K+1}\|\leq\sqrt{64L\widetilde{\Delta}}+3\frac{\widetilde{c}}{\sqrt{G}}+3\sqrt{2c\delta_{\rm byz}}\zeta+3\sqrt{2c\delta_{\rm byz}}\sqrt{\beta}\tilde{z},

from
Lemma

15
we get that

\|\nabla f(x^{K+1})-v^{K+1}\|\leq\sqrt{4L\widetilde{\Delta}}+\frac{3}{2}\frac{\widetilde{c}}{\sqrt{G}}+\frac{3}{2}\sqrt{2c\delta_{\rm byz}}\zeta+\frac{3}{2}\sqrt{2c\delta_{\rm byz}}\sqrt{\beta}\tilde{z},

from
Lemma

14
we get

\frac{1}{G}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{G}}\|\overline{v}^{K+1}-v^{K+1}_{i}\|^{2}\leq 2\zeta^{2}+2\beta\tilde{z}^{2}.

This means that conditions 1-5 in the induction assumption are also verified for the step
K+1
. Since for all
t\in\{0,\dots,K+1\}
inequalities
1
-
7
are verified, we can write for each
t\in\{0,\ldots,K\}
by
Lemmas

13
and
9
the following

\displaystyle\widetilde{\Phi}^{t+1}

\displaystyle=\delta^{t+1}+\frac{\gamma}{\beta}\widetilde{P}^{t+1}

\displaystyle\leq\delta^{t}-\frac{\gamma}{2}\|\nabla f(x^{t})\|^{2}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}-\frac{1}{4\gamma}R^{t}}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}+\gamma\widetilde{P}^{t}}+2c\delta_{\rm byz}\gamma(\zeta^{2}+\beta\tilde{z}^{2})

\displaystyle\;+\;\frac{\gamma}{\beta}\left({\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}(1-\beta)\widetilde{P}^{t}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}+\frac{3L^{2}}{\beta}R^{t}}+\beta^{2}\frac{\widetilde{c}^{2}}{G}+2\beta(1-\beta)\langle\overline{v}^{t}-\nabla f(x^{t+1}),\theta^{t+1}\rangle\right).

Rearranging terms, we get

\displaystyle\widetilde{\Phi}^{t+1}

\displaystyle\leq\delta^{t}-\frac{\gamma}{2}\|\nabla f(x^{t})\|^{2}+\frac{\gamma}{\beta}\widetilde{P}^{t}\left(\beta+1-\beta\right)+2c\delta_{\rm byz}\gamma(\zeta^{2}+\beta\tilde{z}^{2})-\frac{1}{4\gamma}R^{t}\left(1-\frac{12L^{2}}{\beta^{2}}\gamma^{2}\right)+\widetilde{c}^{2}\frac{\gamma\beta}{G}

\displaystyle\quad+2\gamma(1-\beta)\langle\nabla f(x^{t})-\nabla f(x^{t+1}),\theta^{t+1}\rangle.

Using step-size restriction
(ii)
, we get rid of the term with
R^{t}
and obtain

\displaystyle\widetilde{\Phi}^{t+1}

\displaystyle\leq\delta^{t}-\frac{\gamma}{2}\|\nabla f(x^{t})\|^{2}+\frac{\gamma}{\beta}\widetilde{P}^{t}+2c\delta_{\rm byz}\gamma(\zeta^{2}+\beta\tilde{z}^{2})+\widetilde{c}^{2}\frac{\gamma\beta}{G}+2\gamma(1-\beta)\langle\nabla f(x^{t})-\nabla f(x^{t+1}),\theta^{t+1}\rangle.

Now we sum all the inequalities above for
t\in\{0,\dots,K\}
and get

\displaystyle\widetilde{\Phi}^{K+1}

\displaystyle\leq\widetilde{\Phi}^{0}-\frac{\gamma}{2}\sum_{t=0}^{K}\|\nabla f(x^{t})\|^{2}+K\widetilde{c}^{2}\frac{\gamma\beta}{G}+2c\delta_{\rm byz}\gamma(\zeta^{2}+\beta\tilde{z}^{2})K+2\gamma(1-\beta)\sum_{t=0}^{K}\langle\nabla f(x^{t})-\nabla f(x^{t+1}),\theta^{t+1}\rangle.

(111)

Rearranging terms, we get

\displaystyle\frac{\gamma}{2}\sum_{t=0}^{K}\|\nabla f(x^{t})\|^{2}\leq\widetilde{\Phi}^{0}-\widetilde{\Phi}^{K+1}+K\widetilde{c}^{2}\frac{\gamma\beta}{G}+2c\delta_{\rm byz}\gamma(\zeta^{2}+\beta\tilde{z}^{2})K+2\gamma(1-\beta)\sum_{t=0}^{K}\langle\nabla f(x^{t})-\nabla f(x^{t+1}),\theta^{t+1}\rangle.

Taking into account that
\frac{\gamma}{2}\sum_{t=0}^{K}\|\nabla f(x^{t})\|^{2}\geq 0
, we get that the event
\widetilde{E}^{K}\cap\left(\cap_{i\in\mathcal{G}}\overline{\widetilde{\Theta}^{K+1}_{i}}\right)\cap\left(\cap_{i\in\mathcal{G}}\overline{\hat{\Theta}_{i}^{t}}\right)\cap\overline{\widetilde{\Theta}^{K+1}}
implies

\displaystyle\widetilde{\Phi}^{K+1}\leq\widetilde{\Phi}^{0}+K\widetilde{c}^{2}\frac{\gamma\beta}{G}+2c\delta_{\rm byz}\gamma(\zeta^{2}+\beta\tilde{z}^{2})K+2\gamma(1-\beta)\sum_{t=0}^{K}\langle\nabla f(x^{t})-\nabla f(x^{t+1}),\theta^{t+1}\rangle.

Next, we define the following random vector:

\displaystyle\zeta_{5}^{t}\coloneqq\begin{cases}\nabla f(x^{t})-\nabla f(x^{t+1}),&\text{ if }\|\nabla f(x^{t})-\nabla f(x^{t+1})\|\leq L\gamma\left(\sqrt{64L\widetilde{\Delta}}+3\frac{\widetilde{c}}{\sqrt{G}}+3\sqrt{2c\delta_{\rm byz}}\zeta+3\sqrt{2c\delta_{\rm byz}}\sqrt{\beta}\tilde{z}\right)\\
0,&\text{otherwise}\end{cases}.

By definition, the introduced random vector
\zeta_{5}^{t}
is bounded with probability
1
. Moreover, by the definition of
\widetilde{E}^{t}
we get that the event
\widetilde{E}^{K}\cap\overline{\widetilde{\Theta}^{K+1}}\cap\left(\cap_{i\in\mathcal{G}}\overline{\widetilde{\Theta}^{K+1}_{i}}\right)\cap\left(\cap_{i\in\mathcal{G}}\overline{\hat{\Theta}_{i}^{t}}\right)
implies

\displaystyle\zeta_{5}^{t}=\nabla f(x^{t})-\nabla f(x^{t+1}).

Therefore, the event
\widetilde{E}^{K}\cap\overline{\widetilde{\Theta}^{K+1}}\cap\left(\cap_{i\in\mathcal{G}}\overline{\widetilde{\Theta}^{K+1}_{i}}\right)\cap\left(\cap_{i\in\mathcal{G}}\overline{\hat{\Theta}_{i}^{t}}\right)
implies

\displaystyle\widetilde{\Phi}^{K+1}\leq\widetilde{\Phi}^{0}+\underbrace{K\widetilde{\widetilde{c}}^{2}\frac{\gamma\beta}{G}}_{I}+\underbrace{\frac{2\gamma(1-\beta)}{G}\sum_{t=0}^{K}\langle\zeta_{5}^{t},\theta^{t+1}\rangle}_{II}+\underbrace{2c\delta_{\rm byz}\gamma(\zeta^{2}+\beta\tilde{z}^{2})K}_{III}.

Bound of the term I.

Since
4L\gamma\leq\beta
, for the term
I
we have

\displaystyle K\widetilde{c}^{2}\frac{\gamma\beta}{G}\leq K\widetilde{c}^{2}\frac{\beta^{2}}{4LG}.

By choosing
\beta
such that

\beta\leq\left(\frac{4L\widetilde{\Delta}G}{3T\widetilde{c}^{2}}\right)^{1/2},

(112)

we get that

K\widetilde{c}^{2}\frac{\gamma\beta}{G}\leq\frac{\widetilde{\Delta}}{2}.

This bound holds with probability
1.
Note that the worst dependency in the restriction on
\beta
w.r.t.
T
is
\mathcal{O}(\nicefrac{{1}}{{T^{1/2}}})
.

Bound of the term III.

Since
4L\gamma\leq\beta
, for the term
III
we have

\displaystyle 2c\delta_{\rm byz}\gamma(\zeta^{2}+\beta\tilde{z}^{2})K\leq\frac{1}{2L}c\delta_{\rm byz}\beta(\zeta^{2}+\beta\tilde{z}^{2})K.

By choosing
\beta
such that

\beta\leq\min\left\{\frac{L\widetilde{\Delta}}{3c\delta_{\rm byz}\zeta^{2}T},\left(\frac{L\widetilde{\Delta}}{3c\delta_{\rm byz}T\tilde{z}^{2}}\right)^{1/2}\right\},

(113)

we get that

\frac{1}{2L}c\delta_{\rm byz}\beta(\zeta^{2}+\beta\tilde{z}^{2})K\leq\frac{\widetilde{\Delta}}{3}.

This bound holds with probability
1.
Note that the worst dependency in the restriction on
\beta
w.r.t.
T
is
\mathcal{O}(\nicefrac{{1}}{{T}})
.

Bound of the term II.

The bound in this case is obtained by using concentration inequality
Lemma

2
. We define

\sigma^{2}_{8}\coloneqq 4L^{2}\gamma^{2}\cdot\left(\sqrt{64L\widetilde{\Delta}}+3\frac{\widetilde{c}}{\sqrt{G}}+3\sqrt{2c\delta_{\rm byz}}\zeta+3\sqrt{2c\delta_{\rm byz}}\sqrt{\beta}\tilde{z}\right)^{2}\cdot\frac{\sigma^{2}}{G}.

Then we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\exp\left(\left|\frac{4L^{2}\gamma^{2}}{\sigma^{2}_{8}}4(1-\beta)^{2}\langle\zeta^{l}_{5},\theta^{l+1}\rangle^{2}\right|\right)\mid l\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\exp\left(\frac{4L^{2}\gamma^{2}}{\sigma^{2}_{8}}\|\zeta_{5}^{l}\|^{2}\cdot\|\theta^{l+1}\|^{2}\right)\mid l\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\exp\left(\frac{4L^{2}\gamma^{2}}{\sigma^{2}_{8}}\left(\sqrt{64L\widetilde{\Delta}}+3\frac{\widetilde{c}}{\sqrt{G}}+3\sqrt{2c\delta_{\rm byz}}\zeta+3\sqrt{2c\delta_{\rm byz}}\sqrt{\beta}\tilde{z}\right)\cdot\|\theta^{l+1}\|^{2}\right)^{2}\mid l\right].

Since
\theta^{l+1}
is sub-Gaussian with parameter
\frac{\sigma^{2}}{G}
, then we can continue the chain of inequalities above using the definition of
\sigma_{8}^{2}

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\exp\left(\left[4L^{2}\gamma^{2}\cdot\left(\sqrt{64L\widetilde{\Delta}}+3\frac{\widetilde{c}}{\sqrt{G}}+3\sqrt{2c\delta_{\rm byz}}\zeta+3\sqrt{2c\delta_{\rm byz}}\sqrt{\beta}\tilde{z}\right)^{2}\cdot\frac{\sigma^{2}}{G}\right]^{-1}\cdot\right.\right.

\displaystyle\qquad\left.\left.4L^{2}\gamma^{2}\cdot\left(\sqrt{64L\widetilde{\Delta}}+3\frac{\widetilde{c}}{\sqrt{G}}+3\sqrt{2c\delta_{\rm byz}}\zeta+3\sqrt{2c\delta_{\rm byz}}\sqrt{\beta}\tilde{z}\right)^{2}\cdot\|\theta^{l+1}\|^{2}\right)\mid l\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\exp\left(\frac{\|\theta^{l+1}\|^{2}}{\sigma^{2}/G}\right)\right]\leq\exp(1).

Therefore, we have by
Lemma

2
that

\displaystyle\Pr\left[2\gamma(1-\beta)\left\|\sum_{t=0}^{K}\langle\zeta_{5}^{t},\theta^{t+1}\rangle\right\|\right.

\displaystyle\geq\left.(\sqrt{2}+\sqrt{2}b_{1})\sqrt{\sum_{t=0}^{K}4L^{2}\gamma^{2}\frac{\sigma^{2}}{G}\left(\sqrt{64L\widetilde{\Delta}}+3\frac{\widetilde{c}}{\sqrt{G}}+3\sqrt{2c\delta_{\rm byz}}\zeta+3\sqrt{2c\delta_{\rm byz}}\sqrt{\beta}\tilde{z}\right)^{2}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\exp(-\nicefrac{{\widetilde{b}_{1}^{2}}}{{3}})=\frac{\alpha}{6(T+1)},\quad\text{where}\quad\widetilde{b}_{1}^{2}\coloneqq 3\log\left(\frac{6(T+1)}{\alpha}\right)

Using the restrictions
4L\gamma\leq\beta
, we get

\displaystyle(\sqrt{2}+\sqrt{2}b_{1})\sqrt{(K+1)}\cdot\frac{2L\gamma}{\sqrt{G}}\sigma\left(\sqrt{64L\widetilde{\Delta}}+3\frac{\widetilde{c}}{\sqrt{G}}+3\sqrt{2c\delta_{\rm byz}}\zeta+3\sqrt{2c\delta_{\rm byz}}\sqrt{\beta}\tilde{z}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\;

\displaystyle(\sqrt{2}+\sqrt{2}b_{1})\sqrt{(K+1)}\cdot\frac{\beta\sigma}{2L\sqrt{G}}\left(\sqrt{64L\widetilde{\Delta}}+3\frac{\widetilde{c}}{\sqrt{G}}+3\sqrt{2c\delta_{\rm byz}}\zeta+3\sqrt{2c\delta_{\rm byz}}\tilde{z}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\;

\displaystyle\frac{\widetilde{\Delta}}{3}

because we choose
\beta

\displaystyle\beta

\displaystyle\leq\left(\frac{2L\widetilde{\Delta}\sqrt{G}}{3\sqrt{2}(1+\widetilde{b}_{1})\sigma\sqrt{T}\left(\sqrt{64L\widetilde{\Delta}}+3\frac{\widetilde{c}}{\sqrt{G}}+3\sqrt{2c\delta_{\rm byz}}\zeta+3\sqrt{2c\delta_{\rm byz}}\tilde{z}\right)}\right),

(114)

\displaystyle\text{and}\quad K+1\leq T.

This implies

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(2\gamma(1-\beta)\left\|\sum_{t=0}^{K}\langle\zeta_{5,i}^{t},\theta^{t+1}\rangle\right\|\geq\frac{\widetilde{\Delta}}{3}\right)\leq\frac{\alpha}{6(T+1)}.

Note that the worst dependency in the choice of
\beta
w.r.t
T
is
\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}(\nicefrac{{1}}{{T^{1/2}}}).

Final probability.

Therefore, the probability event

\Omega\coloneqq\widetilde{E}^{K}\cap\overline{\widetilde{\Theta}^{K+1}}\cap\left(\cap_{i\in\mathcal{G}}\overline{\widetilde{\Theta}^{K+1}_{i}}\right)\cap\left(\cap_{i\in\mathcal{G}}\overline{\hat{\Theta}_{i}^{K+1}}\right)\cap E_{I}\cap E_{II}\cap E_{III},

where
E_{I}
-
E_{III}
denotes that each of the terms
I,II,
and
III
is smaller than
\frac{\widetilde{\Delta}}{3}
. This implies that

I+II+III\leq\widetilde{\Delta},

i.e., condition
6
in the induction assumption holds. Moreover, this also implies that

\widetilde{\Phi}^{K+1}\leq\widetilde{\Phi}^{0}+\widetilde{\Delta}\leq\widetilde{\Delta}+\widetilde{\Delta}=2\widetilde{\Delta},

i.e., condition
5
in the induction assumption holds. The probability
\mathbb{P}(\widetilde{E}_{K+1})
can be lower bounded as follows

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\widetilde{E}_{K+1})

\displaystyle\geq\mathbb{P}(\Omega)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\left(\widetilde{E}_{K}\cap\overline{\widetilde{\Theta}^{K+1}}\cap\left(\cap_{i\in\mathcal{G}}\overline{\widetilde{\Theta}^{K+1}_{i}}\right)\cap\left(\cap_{i\in\mathcal{G}}\overline{\hat{\Theta}_{i}^{K+1}}\right)\cap E_{I}\cap E_{II}\cap E_{III}\right)

\displaystyle=1-\mathbb{P}\left(\overline{\widetilde{E}}_{K}\cup\widetilde{\Theta}^{K+1}\cup\left(\cup_{i\in\mathcal{G}}\widetilde{\Theta}^{K+1}_{i}\right)\cup\left(\cup_{i\in\mathcal{G}}\hat{\Theta}^{K+1}_{i}\right)\cup\overline{E}_{I}\cup\overline{E}_{II}\cup\overline{E}_{III}\right)

\displaystyle\geq 1-\mathbb{P}(\overline{\widetilde{E}_{K}})-\mathbb{P}(\widetilde{\Theta}^{K+1})-\sum_{i\in\mathcal{G}}\mathbb{P}(\widetilde{\Theta}^{K+1}_{i})-\sum_{i\in\mathcal{G}}\mathbb{P}(\hat{\Theta}^{K+1}_{i})-\underbrace{\mathbb{P}(\overline{E}_{I})}_{=0}-\mathbb{P}(\overline{E}_{II})-\underbrace{\mathbb{P}(\overline{E}_{III})}_{=0}

\displaystyle\geq 1-\frac{\alpha(K+1)}{T+1}-\frac{\alpha}{8(T+1)}-\sum_{i\in\mathcal{G}}\frac{\alpha}{8G(T+1)}-\sum_{i\in\mathcal{G}}\frac{\alpha}{8G(T+1)}-\frac{\alpha}{8(T+1)}

\displaystyle\geq 1-\frac{\alpha(K+2)}{T+1}.

This finalizes the transition step of induction. The result of the theorem follows by setting
K=T-1
. Indeed, from (
70
) we obtain

\displaystyle\frac{\gamma}{2}\sum_{t=0}^{K}\|\nabla f(x^{t})\|^{2}\leq\widetilde{\Phi}^{0}-\widetilde{\Phi}^{K+1}+\widetilde{\Delta}\leq 2\widetilde{\Delta}\Rightarrow\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=0}^{T-1}\|\nabla f(x^{t})\|^{2}\leq\frac{4\widetilde{\Delta}}{\gamma T}.

(115)

Algorithm 4

Safe-DSHB

1:

Input:

x^{0}\in X,
momentum
\beta\in(0,1],
step-size
\gamma>0
,

clipping
\tau>0
, DP-noise variance
\sigma_{\omega}^{2}\geq 0

do

Final rate.

We highlight that we are interested in the functional dependency of the rate on the problem constants. Therefore, in the rest of the proof, we omit using numerical constants. Translating momentum restriction to step-size restriction

\displaystyle\gamma=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{L}\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(\min\left\{1,\underbrace{\left(\frac{L\widetilde{\Delta}G}{T\sigma^{2}}\right)^{1/2}}_{\text{from term I}\penalty\ \eqref{eq:step-size_bound_1_nodp}},\underbrace{\left(\frac{L\widetilde{\Delta}\sqrt{G}}{\sigma\sqrt{T}(\sqrt{L\widetilde{\Delta}}+\sigma/\sqrt{G}+\sqrt{c\delta_{\rm byz}}\zeta+\sqrt{c\delta_{\rm byz}}\sigma}\right)}_{\text{from term II}\penalty\ \eqref{eq:step-size_bound_8_nodp}},\underbrace{\left(\frac{L\widetilde{\Delta}}{c\delta_{\rm byz}T\zeta^{2}}\right)}_{\text{from term III}\penalty\ \eqref{eq:step-size_bound_4_nodp}},\right\}\right),

(116)

We plug in the restrictions (
116
) on the step-size into (
115
) and obtain the rate

\displaystyle\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=0}^{T-1}\|\nabla f(x^{t})\|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{L\widetilde{\Delta}}{T}\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(1+\left(\frac{T\sigma^{2}}{L\widetilde{\Delta}G}\right)^{1/2}+\frac{\sigma\sqrt{T}(\sqrt{L\widetilde{\Delta}}+\sigma/\sqrt{G}+\sqrt{c\delta_{\rm byz}}\zeta+\sqrt{c\delta_{\rm byz}}\sigma)}{L\widetilde{\Delta}\sqrt{G}}+\frac{c\delta_{\rm byz}T\zeta^{2}}{L\widetilde{\Delta}}\right)

\displaystyle=\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(\frac{L\widetilde{\Delta}}{T}+\left(\frac{\sigma^{2}L\widetilde{\Delta}}{GT}\right)^{1/2}+\frac{\sigma(\sqrt{L\widetilde{\Delta}}+\sigma/\sqrt{G}+\sqrt{c\delta_{\rm byz}}\zeta+\sqrt{c\delta_{\rm byz}}\sigma)}{\sqrt{GT}}+c\delta_{\rm byz}\zeta^{2}\right).

(117)

It remains to provide a valid upper bound
\widetilde{\Delta}
on
\widetilde{\Phi}_{0}
. Since we initialize
v_{i}^{0}=0
, then

\widetilde{\Phi}^{0}=F^{0}+\frac{\gamma}{\beta}\|\nabla f(x^{0})\|^{2}\leq F^{0}+\frac{1}{4L}\cdot 2L(f(x^{0})-f^{\star})=2F^{0}.

This concludes the proof.

∎

Appendix F
Additional Experiments and Training Details

Our implementation builds on the codebase of
50
to simulate a distributed environment, while the attack implementations are adopted from
43
. It can be found via the link
https://anonymous.4open.science/r/ByzClip21SGD2M
.

F.1
Training Details

In our experiments, we compare
Byz-Clip21-SGD2M
against
Byz-Clip-SGD
and
Safe-DSHB

[
9
]
, outlined in
Algorithm

3
and
Algorithm

4
, respectively. Note that
Byz-Clip-SGD
is a variant of
DP-SGD
in which a robust aggregation rule replaces server-side averaging. Since
DP-SGD
is state-of-the-art for private learning, this modification (
Algorithm

3
) serves as our first baseline. The method of
9
originally performs per-example clipping (line 5); to align with our analysis under
\sigma
-sub-Gaussian noise, i.e., without assuming a finite-sum structure for
f_{i}
, we instead clip the stochastic gradient.

In experiments of
Section

6
, we use a coupling of Nearest Neighbor Mixing (
NNM
)
[
7
]
and Coordinate Median (
CM
)
[
21
]
as an aggregation rule, while Gaussian mechanism utilizes
\delta=4\cdot 10^{-4}.

F.2
Label Flipping Attack

We evaluate
Byz-Clip21-SGD2M
,
Byz-Clip-SGD
, and
Safe-DSHB
on CNN and MLP models under a label-flipping attack
[
82
]
. MNIST is evenly partitioned across
n=25
clients; Byzantine clients flip labels as
y\to 9-y
. We vary the number of Byzantine clients and the privacy budget
\varepsilon\in\{3,8,13,18,23\}
, fixing
\delta=4\cdot 10^{-4}
. Hyperparameter tuning follows
Section

6
. All methods use mini-batch gradients with a batch size
32
and no privacy amplification by sub-sampling. We use a coupling of
NNM
and
CM
as a server-side aggregation rule. All algorithms are run for
60
epochs.

Figure

2
summarizes the results. Unlike under the
IPM
attack, performance degrades for all methods as the number of Byzantine clients grows. Even so,
Byz-Clip21-SGD2M
typically achieves the best test accuracy, aligning with our theory. In most settings, the margin is substantial, highlighting the effectiveness of the proposed algorithm for private training.

F.3
Amplification by Sub-sampling

Algorithm 5

Byz-Clip21-SGD2M+

1:

Input:

x^{0}\in X,
momentum parameters
\beta,\hat{\beta}\in(0,1],
step-size
\gamma>0
,
g_{i}^{0}=m_{i}^{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{d},
clipping parameters
\tau_{i},\tau_{o}>0
, DP-noise variance
\sigma_{\omega}^{2}\geq 0

2:

for

t=0,\ldots,T-1

do

3:

x^{t+1}=x^{t}-\gamma g^{t}

4:

for

i\in\mathcal{G}

do

5:

Sample
\mathcal{S}_{i}^{t+1}\sim{\rm Unif}[m]
of cardinality
S

6:

\omega_{i}^{t+1}\sim\mathcal{N}(0,\sigma_{\omega}^{2}\mathbf{I})

7:

v_{i}^{t+1}=(1-\beta)v_{i}^{t}+\frac{\beta}{S}\sum_{j\in\mathcal{S}_{i}^{t+1}}(\clip_{\tau_{i}}(\nabla f_{ij}(x^{t+1}))+\omega_{i}^{t+1})

8:

c_{i}^{t+1}=\clip_{\tau}(v_{i}^{t+1}-g_{i}^{t})

9:

g_{i}^{t+1}=g_{i}^{t}+\hat{\beta}\clip_{\tau_{o}}(v_{i}^{t+1}-g_{i}^{t})

10:

end

for

11:

for

i\in\mathcal{B}

do

12:

c_{i}^{t+1}=(*)
sends arbitrary vector

13:

end

for

14:

m_{i}^{t+1}=m_{i}^{t}+\hat{\beta}c_{i}^{t+1}

15:

g^{t+1}={\rm RAgg}(m_{1}^{t+1},\dots,m_{n}^{t+1})

16:

end

for

We next consider a modified variant of
Byz-Clip21-SGD2M
(see
Algorithm

5
), which we call
Byz-Clip21-SGD2M+
. Unlike the population setting considered in the main body, each client now holds a finite dataset of size
m
with a local objective

f(x)=\frac{1}{G}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{G}}f_{i}(x),\quad f_{i}(x)=\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}f_{ij}(x).

At iteration
t
, client
i\in\mathcal{G}
samples a mini-batch
\mathcal{S}^{t+1}_{i}
of size
S
, averages example-wise clipped stochastic gradients, and updates its momentum buffer using a noised version of this average (line 7 in
Algorithm

5
). This modification enables privacy amplification through sub-sampling, as each per-example gradient is protected via the Gaussian mechanism, and local DP guarantees are implied by post-processing. Consequently, the required DP noise can be reduced to

\sigma_{\omega}=\frac{S}{m}\cdot\frac{\tau}{\varepsilon}\sqrt{T\log\frac{1}{\delta}},

following
1
. We highlight that
Byz-Clip21-SGD2M+
has two clipping operators. In the experiments, we vary the inner one (line 7) while the outer one is fixed
\tau_{o}=1
(line 9). We compare this enhanced version of
Byz-Clip21-SGD2M
against
Byz-Clip-SGD
and original
Safe-DSHB
, where example-wise clipping is also incorporated into the algorithm design.

We compare all methods under the previous setup: CNN/MLP training on MNIST with a stronger privacy budget
\varepsilon\in\{0.1,0.3,1,3\}
and a label-flipping attack. We split the training set across
n=25
clients equally. We tune the learning rate over
\{10,1,0.1,0.01\}
and the clipping threshold over
\{1,0.3,0.1,0.03,0.01\}
(this corresponds to
\tau_{i}
in
Byz-Clip21-SGD2M+
). For
Safe-DSHB
and
Byz-Clip21-SGD2M+
we set
\beta=0.1
as before; for
Byz-Clip21-SGD2M+
we use
\tau_{o}=1
and
\hat{\beta}=0.01
. To defend against Byzantine clients, we employ a hybrid aggregation rule on the server that combines
NNM
and
CM
. We decrease the number of epochs to
30
.

Results in
Figure

F.1
(bottom line) show that with
1
or
5
Byzantine clients, the methods are broadly competitive when training MLP model, with
Safe-DSHB
slightly leading at
\varepsilon=0.1
. With
10
Byzantines,
Safe-DSHB
and
Byz-Clip-SGD
edge out
Byz-Clip21-SGD2M+
at
\varepsilon=0.1
, whereas for
\varepsilon\in\{0.3,1,3\}

Byz-Clip21-SGD2M+
clearly outperforms both baselines.

For the CNN model (
Figure

F.1
, top row), the trend is similar:
Byz-Clip-SGD
and
Safe-DSHB
perform slightly better at
\varepsilon=0.1
, whereas
Byz-Clip21-SGD2M+
is marginally more effective when the number of Byzantine clients is large.

Experiments on CIFAR10
[
64
]
with the CNN model showed no significant difference between
Byz-Clip21-SGD2M+
and the baselines; therefore, we do not report these results.

While we do not provide convergence guarantees for
Byz-Clip21-SGD2M+
, our experiments show that it delivers competitive performance against other baselines when amplification by sub-sampling is enabled. A theoretical analysis is deferred to future work since the example-wise clipping makes the analysis substantially more involved than for
Byz-Clip21-SGD2M
.

Figure F.1
:
Performance of
Byz-Clip21-SGD2M+
,
Byz-Clip-SGD
, and
Safe-DSHB
when training CNN (top line) and MLP (bottom line) models on the MNIST dataset for different numbers of Byzantine clients and privacy budgets, when Byzantine clients use a label flipping attack, when amplification by sub-sampling is done.
